(A.43) |
Theory 4.5 states that under a specific schedule for the hyperparameters and a gradual reduction in quantization error, Quantized Adam achieves the same convergence rate as its full-precision counterpart. We prove this by performing a detailed asymptotic analysis of each term in the main bound from Theorem 4.5 as the total number of iterations T→∞T\to\infty.
However, to perform a precise asymptotic analysis and derive the tightest possible convergence rate from our framework, we will now analyze the order of each component from the more detailed bound in A.41:
|
𝔼⁡[‖∇F​(vec​(𝐖)τ)‖22]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{W})_{\tau})\|_{2}^{2}\right] |
≤2​(1+qG)​R​F0−F∗η​T~⏟Term 1\displaystyle\leq \underbrace{2(1+q_{G})R\frac{F_{0}-F_{*}}{\eta\tilde{T}}}_{\text{Term 1}} |
|
|
|
+12​d​((1+qG)​R)2​1−β1T~​(1−β1/β2)3/2​1−β2​(ln⁡(1+((1+qG)​R)2ϵ⁡(1−β2))−T​ln⁡(β2))⏟Term 2\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{12d((1+q_{G})R)^{2}\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}{\tilde{T}(1-\beta_{1}/\beta_{2})^{3/2}\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\left(\ln\left(1+\frac{((1+q_{G})R)^{2}}{\epsilon(1-\beta_{2})}\right)-T\ln(\beta_{2})\right)}_{\text{Term 2}} |
|
|
|
+ET~​(ln⁡(1+((1+qG)​R)2ϵ⁡(1−β2​(1−qV)))−T​ln⁡(β2​(1−qV)))⏟Term 3\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{E}{\tilde{T}}\left(\ln\left(1+\frac{((1+q_{G})R)^{2}}{\epsilon(1-\beta_{2}(1-q_{V}))}\right)-T\ln(\beta_{2}(1-q_{V}))\right)}_{\text{Term 3}} |
|
|
|
+Q⁡(T)T~⏟Term 4+2​(1+qG)​d​TT~​ϵ⁡(1−β2)​(qG​R3+L​qW​R2​‖vec⁡(𝐖0)‖2)⏟Term 5\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{Q(T)}{\tilde{T}}}_{\text{Term 4}}+\underbrace{\frac{2(1+q_{G})dT}{\tilde{T}\sqrt{\epsilon(1-\beta_{2})}}\left(q_{G}R^{3}+Lq_{W}R^{2}\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{W}_{0})\|_{2}\right)}_{\text{Term 5}} |
|
|
|
+(1−β1)​d32​η​L​qW​(1+qG)​R2​T2T~​ϵ​(1−β2)​1−β12​(1+qM)2β2​(1−qV)⏟Term 6.\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{(1-\beta_{1})d^{\frac{3}{2}}\eta Lq_{W}(1+q_{G})R^{2}T^{2}}{\tilde{T}\sqrt{\epsilon}(1-\beta_{2})\sqrt{1-\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}(1+q_{M})^{2}}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}}}}_{\text{Term 6}}. |
|
Our proof strategy is to analyze the asymptotic order of each term under the following scaling assumptions.
Scaling Assumptions.
We adopt the scaling assumptions provided in the Theorem:
•
Quantization Error Schedules: The quantization errors are annealed over time such that qG=𝒪⁡(T−1)q_{G}=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1}), qM=𝒪⁡(T−1)q_{M}=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1}), qW=𝒪⁡(T−2)q_{W}=\mathcal{O}(T^{-2}), and qV=𝒪⁡(T−2)q_{V}=\mathcal{O}(T^{-2}).
•
Adam Hyperparameters: The learning rate and second-moment decay are set as η=Θ(T−1/2)\eta=\Theta(T^{-1/2}) and 1−β2=Θ⁡(1/T)1-\beta_{2}=\Theta(1/T), while β1\beta_{1} is treated as a constant.
Asymptotic Analysis of Bound Terms.
We now analyze the order of magnitude for each of the six terms.
Term 1 (Initial Condition Term): This term is given by T1=2​(1+qG)​R​F0−F∗η​T~T_{1}=2(1+q_{G})R\frac{F_{0}-F_{*}}{\eta\tilde{T}}.
We analyze the components of its denominator. The effective number of iterations is T~=T−β11−β1=Θ⁡(T)\tilde{T}=T-\frac{\beta_{1}}{1-\beta_{1}}=\Theta(T). The learning rate scales as η=Θ(T−1/2)\eta=\Theta(T^{-1/2}). The denominator thus scales as ηT~=Θ(T−1/2)Θ(T)=Θ(T1/2)\eta\tilde{T}=\Theta(T^{-1/2})\Theta(T)=\Theta(T^{1/2}). Since all other quantities are constants and qG→0q_{G}\to 0, the entire term scales as:
|
T1=Θ(1η​T~)=Θ(1T1/2)=Θ(T−1/2).\displaystyle T_{1}=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{\eta\tilde{T}}\right)=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{T^{1/2}}\right)=\Theta(T^{-1/2}). |
|
Term 2 (First Logarithmic Term): This term is:
|
T2=12​d​((1+qG)​R)2​1−β1T~​(1−β1/β2)3/2​1−β2​(ln⁡(1+((1+qG)​R)2ϵ⁡(1−β2))−T​ln⁡(β2))\displaystyle T_{2}=\frac{12d((1+q_{G})R)^{2}\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}{\tilde{T}(1-\beta_{1}/\beta_{2})^{3/2}\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\left(\ln\left(1+\frac{((1+q_{G})R)^{2}}{\epsilon(1-\beta_{2})}\right)-T\ln(\beta_{2})\right) |
|
The leading fraction’s order is determined by its denominator, T~​1−β2\tilde{T}\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}. With T~=Θ⁡(T)\tilde{T}=\Theta(T) and 1−β2=Θ⁡(1/T)1-\beta_{2}=\Theta(1/T), we have 1−β2=Θ(T−1/2)\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}=\Theta(T^{-1/2}). Thus, the fraction scales as Θ(1T⋅T−1/2)=Θ(T−1/2)\Theta(\frac{1}{T\cdot T^{-1/2}})=\Theta(T^{-1/2}). The term in the parenthesis scales as ln⁡(1+Θ⁡(T))−Θ⁡(1)=Θ⁡(ln⁡T)\ln(1+\Theta(T))-\Theta(1)=\Theta(\ln T). The overall order is:
|
T2=Θ(1T~​1−β2)⋅Θ(lnT)=Θ(T−1/2)⋅Θ(lnT)=Θ(ln⁡TT).\displaystyle T_{2}=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{T}\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\right)\cdot\Theta(\ln T)=\Theta\left(T^{-1/2}\right)\cdot\Theta(\ln T)=\Theta\left(\frac{\ln T}{\sqrt{T}}\right). |
|
Term 3 (Second Logarithmic Term): This term is T3=ET~​(ln⁡(…)−T​ln⁡(…))T_{3}=\frac{E}{\tilde{T}}\left(\ln(...)-T\ln(...)\right).
First, we determine the asymptotic order of EE, which is defined as:
|
E=d​η​L​(1+qG)​R​(1−β1)2(1−β1​(1+qM))​(1−β1​(1+qM)β2​(1−qV))​(1−β2)+2​d​η2​L2​β1​(1−β1)(1−β1​(1+qM))​(1−β1​(1+qM)β2​(1−qV))​(1−β2)32.E=\frac{d\eta L(1+q_{G})R(1-\beta_{1})^{2}}{(1-\beta_{1}(1+q_{M}))(1-\frac{\beta_{1}(1+q_{M})}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})})(1-\beta_{2})}+\frac{2d\eta^{2}L^{2}\beta_{1}(1-\beta_{1})}{(1-\beta_{1}(1+q_{M}))(1-\frac{\beta_{1}(1+q_{M})}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})})(1-\beta_{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}. |
|
For the first part of EE, the numerator scales as η=Θ(T−1/2)\eta=\Theta(T^{-1/2}) and the denominator is dominated by (1−β2)=Θ⁡(T−1)(1-\beta_{2})=\Theta(T^{-1}). This part is Θ(T−1/2)/Θ(T−1)=Θ(T1/2)\Theta(T^{-1/2})/\Theta(T^{-1})=\Theta(T^{1/2}). For the second part, the numerator scales as η2=Θ⁡(T−1)\eta^{2}=\Theta(T^{-1}) and the denominator is dominated by (1−β2)3/2=Θ(T−3/2)(1-\beta_{2})^{3/2}=\Theta(T^{-3/2}). This part is Θ(T−1)/Θ(T−3/2)=Θ(T1/2)\Theta(T^{-1})/\Theta(T^{-3/2})=\Theta(T^{1/2}). Thus, E=Θ⁡(T1/2)E=\Theta(T^{1/2}). The logarithmic part scales as Θ⁡(ln⁡T)\Theta(\ln T), so the entire term scales as:
|
T3=Θ⁡(ET~)⋅Θ⁡(ln⁡T)=Θ⁡(T1/2T)⋅Θ⁡(ln⁡T)=Θ⁡(ln⁡TT).\displaystyle T_{3}=\Theta\left(\frac{E}{\tilde{T}}\right)\cdot\Theta(\ln T)=\Theta\left(\frac{T^{1/2}}{T}\right)\cdot\Theta(\ln T)=\Theta\left(\frac{\ln T}{\sqrt{T}}\right). |
|
Term 4 (Moment Quantization Error): We rewrite this term as:
|
T4=2​(1+qG)​d​R2​T​(1−β1)T~​1−β2​Q,\displaystyle T_{4}=\frac{2(1+q_{G})dR^{2}T(1-\beta_{1})}{\tilde{T}\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}Q, |
|
where Q=qM⋅r′​(1+r′)(1+qM)​(1−r′)3/2+11−β12β2​(1−qV)−1T​11−β12β2​(1−qV)​(1−qVqV​(1−(1−qV)T))Q=q_{M}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{r^{\prime}(1+r^{\prime})}}{(1+q_{M})(1-r^{\prime})^{3/2}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}}}-\frac{1}{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}}}\left(\frac{1-q_{V}}{q_{V}}(1-(1-q_{V})^{T})\right).
Our goal is to show that T4=𝒪(T−1/2)T_{4}=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1/2}).
First, the pre-factor has an asymptotic order of:
|
2​(1+qG)​d​R2​T​(1−β1)T~​1−β2=Θ⁡(TT⋅T−1/2)=Θ⁡(T1/2).\displaystyle\frac{2(1+q_{G})dR^{2}T(1-\beta_{1})}{\tilde{T}\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}=\Theta\left(\frac{T}{T\cdot T^{-1/2}}\right)=\Theta(T^{1/2}). |
|
The core of the analysis thus lies in determining the order of QQ. We can rewrite QQ by combining its second and third components:
|
Q=qM⋅r′​(1+r′)(1+qM)​(1−r′)3/2+11−β12β2​(1−qV)​[1−1T​(1−qVqV​(1−(1−qV)T))].\displaystyle Q=q_{M}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{r^{\prime}(1+r^{\prime})}}{(1+q_{M})(1-r^{\prime})^{3/2}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}}}\left[1-\frac{1}{T}\left(\frac{1-q_{V}}{q_{V}}(1-(1-q_{V})^{T})\right)\right]. |
|
The first part of QQ is clearly 𝒪⁡(qM)=𝒪⁡(T−1)\mathcal{O}(q_{M})=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1}). The common factor in the second part, 11−…\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\dots}}, converges to a constant as T→∞T\to\infty, so it is 𝒪⁡(1)\mathcal{O}(1). The analysis therefore simplifies to finding the order of the bracketed term.
Let x=qV=𝒪⁡(T−2)x=q_{V}=\mathcal{O}(T^{-2}). We perform a Taylor expansion on (1−x)T(1-x)^{T}:
|
(1−x)T=1−T​x+T⁡(T−1)2​x2+𝒪⁡(T3​x3).\displaystyle(1-x)^{T}=1-Tx+\frac{T(T-1)}{2}x^{2}+\mathcal{O}(T^{3}x^{3}). |
|
This allows us to analyze the term inside the bracket:
|
1−1T​(1−xx​(1−(1−x)T))\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{T}\left(\frac{1-x}{x}(1-(1-x)^{T})\right) |
=1−1T​1−xx​(T​x−T⁡(T−1)2​x2+𝒪⁡(T3​x3))\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{T}\frac{1-x}{x}\left(Tx-\frac{T(T-1)}{2}x^{2}+\mathcal{O}(T^{3}x^{3})\right) |
|
|
|
=1−1T​(1−x)​(T−T⁡(T−1)2​x+𝒪⁡(T3​x2))\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{T}(1-x)\left(T-\frac{T(T-1)}{2}x+\mathcal{O}(T^{3}x^{2})\right) |
|
|
|
=1−1T​(T−T⁡(T−1)2​x−T​x+𝒪⁡(T2​x2))\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{T}\left(T-\frac{T(T-1)}{2}x-Tx+\mathcal{O}(T^{2}x^{2})\right) |
|
|
|
=1−(1−T⁡(T+1)2​T​x+𝒪⁡(T2​x2))\displaystyle=1-\left(1-\frac{T(T+1)}{2T}x+\mathcal{O}(T^{2}x^{2})\right) |
|
|
|
=T+12​x−𝒪⁡(T2​x2).\displaystyle=\frac{T+1}{2}x-\mathcal{O}(T^{2}x^{2}). |
|
Substituting back x=qV=𝒪⁡(T−2)x=q_{V}=\mathcal{O}(T^{-2}), the bracketed term has an order of:
|
𝒪⁡(T⋅qV)=𝒪⁡(T⋅T−2)=𝒪⁡(T−1).\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(T\cdot q_{V})=\mathcal{O}(T\cdot T^{-2})=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1}). |
|
Therefore, the entire second component of QQ is 𝒪⁡(1)⋅𝒪⁡(T−1)=𝒪⁡(T−1)\mathcal{O}(1)\cdot\mathcal{O}(T^{-1})=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1}).
Combining both components of QQ, we find its overall order:
|
Q=𝒪⁡(T−1)+𝒪⁡(T−1)=𝒪⁡(T−1).\displaystyle Q=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1})+\mathcal{O}(T^{-1})=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1}). |
|
Finally, we compute the order of Term 4 by combining the pre-factor and QQ:
|
T4=Θ(T1/2)⋅𝒪(T−1)=𝒪(T−1/2).\displaystyle T_{4}=\Theta(T^{1/2})\cdot\mathcal{O}(T^{-1})=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1/2}). |
|
Term 5 (Initial W/G Quantization Error): This term is:
|
T5=2​(1+qG)​d​TT~​ϵ⁡(1−β2)​(qG​R3+L​qW​R2​‖vec⁡(𝐖0)‖2)\displaystyle T_{5}=\frac{2(1+q_{G})dT}{\tilde{T}\sqrt{\epsilon(1-\beta_{2})}}\left(q_{G}R^{3}+Lq_{W}R^{2}\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{W}_{0})\|_{2}\right) |
|
The leading fraction scales as TT~​1−β2=Θ⁡(T)Θ(T)Θ(T−1/2)=Θ⁡(T1/2)\frac{T}{\tilde{T}\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}=\frac{\Theta(T)}{\Theta(T)\Theta(T^{-1/2})}=\Theta(T^{1/2}). The parenthesis scales with its dominant term qG=𝒪⁡(T−1)q_{G}=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1}). The total order is:
|
T5=Θ(T1/2)⋅𝒪(qG+qW)=Θ(T1/2)⋅(𝒪(T−1)+𝒪(T−2))=𝒪(T−1/2).\displaystyle T_{5}=\Theta(T^{1/2})\cdot\mathcal{O}(q_{G}+q_{W})=\Theta(T^{1/2})\cdot(\mathcal{O}(T^{-1})+\mathcal{O}(T^{-2}))=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1/2}). |
|
Term 6 (Weight Growth Quantization Error): This term is T6=(1−β1)​d32​η​L​qW​(1+qG)​R2​T2T~​ϵ​(1−β2)​1−β12​(1+qM)2β2​(1−qV)T_{6}=\frac{(1-\beta_{1})d^{\frac{3}{2}}\eta Lq_{W}(1+q_{G})R^{2}T^{2}}{\tilde{T}\sqrt{\epsilon}(1-\beta_{2})\sqrt{1-\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}(1+q_{M})^{2}}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}}}.
First, we analyze 11−β12​(1+qM)2β2​(1−qV)\sqrt{\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}(1+q_{M})^{2}}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}}}. As T→∞T\to\infty, the denominator converges to the constant 1−β121-\beta_{1}^{2}, so its contribution is 𝒪⁡(1)\mathcal{O}(1). The term’s order is determined by the scaling of its other components: η=Θ(T−1/2)\eta=\Theta(T^{-1/2}), qW=𝒪⁡(T−2)q_{W}=\mathcal{O}(T^{-2}), T~=Θ⁡(T)\tilde{T}=\Theta(T), and (1−β2)=Θ⁡(T−1)(1-\beta_{2})=\Theta(T^{-1}). The total order is:
|
T6=Θ(η⋅qW⋅T2T~⋅(1−β2))=Θ(T−1/2⋅T−2⋅T2T⋅T−1)=Θ(T−1/2).\displaystyle T_{6}=\Theta\left(\frac{\eta\cdot q_{W}\cdot T^{2}}{\tilde{T}\cdot(1-\beta_{2})}\right)=\Theta\left(\frac{T^{-1/2}\cdot T^{-2}\cdot T^{2}}{T\cdot T^{-1}}\right)=\Theta(T^{-1/2}). |
|
Conclusion.
By comparing the asymptotic orders of all terms, we identify those that converge to zero at the slowest rate, as they will dominate the overall convergence bound. The orders are:
•
Term 1, 4, 5, 6: 𝒪(T−1/2)\mathcal{O}(T^{-1/2}) or Θ(T−1/2)\Theta(T^{-1/2}).
•
Term 2, 3: Θ(T−1/2lnT)\Theta(T^{-1/2}\ln T).
The dominant terms are the second and third, which are of order Θ(T−1/2lnT)\Theta(T^{-1/2}\ln T). These terms form the bottleneck that determines the overall convergence rate. Thus, under the specified parameter schedule, the expected squared gradient norm converges to zero at the following rate:
|
𝔼⁡[‖∇F​(𝐰τ)‖22]=Θ⁡(ln⁡TT)=𝒪~​(1T).\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{\tau})\|_{2}^{2}\right]=\Theta\left(\frac{\ln T}{\sqrt{T}}\right)=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right). |
|
This matches the known convergence rate for full-precision Adam.
Furthermore, we derive the convergence rate for the expected gradient norm, 𝔼⁡[‖∇F​(𝐰τ)‖2]\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{\tau})\|_{2}\right], from the rate of its squared value. We use Jensen’s inequality, which states that for a convex function ϕ\phi and a random variable XX, ϕ⁡(𝔼⁡[X])≤𝔼⁡[ϕ⁡(X)]\phi(\mathbb{E}[X])\leq\mathbb{E}[\phi(X)].
Let the random variable be X=‖∇F​(𝐰τ)‖2X=\|\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{\tau})\|_{2} and the convex function be ϕ⁡(x)=x2\phi(x)=x^{2}. Applying Jensen’s inequality yields:
|
(𝔼⁡[‖∇F​(𝐰τ)‖2])2≤𝔼⁡[‖∇F​(𝐰τ)‖22].\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{\tau})\|_{2}\right]\right)^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{\tau})\|_{2}^{2}\right]. |
|
By taking the square root of both sides, we obtain a bound on the expected norm:
|
𝔼⁡[‖∇F​(𝐰τ)‖2]≤𝔼⁡[‖∇F​(𝐰τ)‖22].\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{\tau})\|_{2}\right]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{\tau})\|_{2}^{2}\right]}. |
|
Substituting our previously derived convergence rate:
|
𝔼⁡[‖∇F​(𝐰τ)‖2]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{\tau})\|_{2}\right] |
≤𝒪~​(1T)\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)} |
|
|
|
=𝒪~​(T−1/2)\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\sqrt{T^{-1/2}}\right) |
|
|
|
=𝒪~(T−1/4).\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(T^{-1/4}\right). |
|
Thus, the expected gradient norm converges to zero at a rate of 𝒪~(T−1/4)\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(T^{-1/4}). This finalizes the proof of the theorem.
A.3 Proof of Lemma A.2
Lemma A.2 (The value range of vt,iv_{t,i} and the upper bound of |1ϵ+vt,i−1ϵ+vt,i′||\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v_{t,i}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}}|).
Let L​Bt,i=∑k=0tβ2t−k​(1−qV)t−k​(∇ifk​(𝐰k−1)+δk,i)2LB_{t,i}=\sum_{k=0}^{t}\beta_{2}^{t-k}(1-q_{V})^{t-k}(\nabla_{i}f_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k-1})+\delta_{k,i})^{2} and U​Bt,i=∑k=0tβ2t−k​(1+qV)t−k​(∇ifk​(𝐰k−1)+δk,i)2UB_{t,i}=\sum_{k=0}^{t}\beta_{2}^{t-k}(1+q_{V})^{t-k}(\nabla_{i}f_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k-1})+\delta_{k,i})^{2}. We have:
|
∑k=0tβ2t−k​(1−qV)t−k​(∇ifk​(𝐰k−1)+δk,i)2≤vt,i≤∑k=0tβ2t−k​(1+qV)t−k​(∇ifk​(𝐰k−1)+δk,i)2\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{t}\beta_{2}^{t-k}(1-q_{V})^{t-k}(\nabla_{i}f_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k-1})+\delta_{k,i})^{2}\leq v_{t,i}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{t}\beta_{2}^{t-k}(1+q_{V})^{t-k}(\nabla_{i}f_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k-1})+\delta_{k,i})^{2} |
|
(A.44) |
|
|1ϵ+vt,i−1ϵ+vt,i′|≤max⁡{1ϵ+L​Bt,i−1ϵ+vt,i′,1ϵ+vt,i′−1ϵ+U​Bt,i}\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v_{t,i}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}}\right|\leq\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+LB_{t,i}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+UB_{t,i}}}\right\} |
|
(A.45) |
Proof.
The proof consists of two parts.
Part 1: Bounding vt,iv_{t,i}
The update rule for the second moment estimate is vt,i=β2​(vt−1,i+θt−1,i)+(∇ift​(𝐰t−1)+δt,i)2v_{t,i}=\beta_{2}(v_{t-1,i}+\theta_{t-1,i})+(\nabla_{i}f_{t}(\mathbf{w}_{t-1})+\delta_{t,i})^{2}. The quantization noise is assumed to be a relative error, bounded by |θt−1,i|≤qV​|vt−1,i||\theta_{t-1,i}|\leq q_{V}|v_{t-1,i}|. This implies that (1−qV)​vt−1,i≤vt−1,i+θt−1,i≤(1+qV)​vt−1,i(1-q_{V})v_{t-1,i}\leq v_{t-1,i}+\theta_{t-1,i}\leq(1+q_{V})v_{t-1,i}.
Applying this to the update rule, we can establish the lower bound by recursively unrolling the inequality:
|
vt,i\displaystyle v_{t,i} |
≥β2​(1−qV)​vt−1,i+(∇ift​(𝐰t−1)+δt,i)2\displaystyle\geq\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})v_{t-1,i}+(\nabla_{i}f_{t}(\mathbf{w}_{t-1})+\delta_{t,i})^{2} |
|
|
|
≥β2​(1−qV)​[β2​(1−qV)​vt−2,i+(∇ift−1​(𝐰t−2)+δt−1,i)2]+(∇ift​(𝐰t−1)+δt,i)2\displaystyle\geq\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})\left[\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})v_{t-2,i}+(\nabla_{i}f_{t-1}(\mathbf{w}_{t-2})+\delta_{t-1,i})^{2}\right]+(\nabla_{i}f_{t}(\mathbf{w}_{t-1})+\delta_{t,i})^{2} |
|
|
|
=…\displaystyle=\dots |
|
|
|
=∑k=0tβ2t−k​(1−qV)t−k​(∇ifk​(𝐰k−1)+δk,i)2\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{t}\beta_{2}^{t-k}(1-q_{V})^{t-k}(\nabla_{i}f_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k-1})+\delta_{k,i})^{2} |
|
(A.46) |
Similarly, we can establish the upper bound:
|
vt,i\displaystyle v_{t,i} |
≤β2​(1+qV)​vt−1,i+(∇ift​(𝐰t−1)+δt,i)2\displaystyle\leq\beta_{2}(1+q_{V})v_{t-1,i}+(\nabla_{i}f_{t}(\mathbf{w}_{t-1})+\delta_{t,i})^{2} |
|
|
|
=∑k=0tβ2t−k​(1+qV)t−k​(∇ifk​(𝐰k−1)+δk,i)2\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{t}\beta_{2}^{t-k}(1+q_{V})^{t-k}(\nabla_{i}f_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k-1})+\delta_{k,i})^{2} |
|
(A.47) |
This completes the proof of the first statement in the lemma.
Part 2: Bounding the difference of the inverse square roots
Let vt,i′v^{\prime}_{t,i} be the idealized second moment estimate, updated without the quantization noise θ\theta. Its explicit form is:
|
vt,i′=∑k=0tβ2t−k​(∇ifk​(𝐰k−1)+δk,i)2\displaystyle v^{\prime}_{t,i}=\sum_{k=0}^{t}\beta_{2}^{t-k}(\nabla_{i}f_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k-1})+\delta_{k,i})^{2} |
|
(A.48) |
From Part 1, we know that vt,iv_{t,i} is in the interval [L​Bt,i,U​Bt,i][LB_{t,i},UB_{t,i}], where L​Bt,iLB_{t,i} and U​Bt,iUB_{t,i} are the bounds established.
Now, we compare vt,i′v^{\prime}_{t,i} with these bounds. Since 0<β2<10<\beta_{2}<1 and we assume 0<qV<10<q_{V}<1, we have β2​(1−qV)<β2<β2​(1+qV)\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})<\beta_{2}<\beta_{2}(1+q_{V}). This implies a term-by-term inequality, leading to:
|
L​Bt,i<vt,i′<U​Bt,i\displaystyle LB_{t,i}<v^{\prime}_{t,i}<UB_{t,i} |
|
(A.49) |
Consider the function f⁡(y)=1/ϵ+yf(y)=1/\sqrt{\epsilon+y} for y≥0y\geq 0. This function is monotonically decreasing and convex. The value vt,iv_{t,i} lies in the interval [L​Bt,i,U​Bt,i][LB_{t,i},UB_{t,i}], and vt,i′v^{\prime}_{t,i} is a point within this interval. The maximum absolute difference |f⁡(vt,i)−f⁡(vt,i′)||f(v_{t,i})-f(v^{\prime}_{t,i})| must occur when vt,iv_{t,i} is at one of the endpoints of the interval. Therefore, we can bound the difference as:
|
|1ϵ+vt,i−1ϵ+vt,i′|\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v_{t,i}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}}\right| |
≤max⁡{|1ϵ+L​Bt,i−1ϵ+vt,i′|,|1ϵ+U​Bt,i−1ϵ+vt,i′|}\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+LB_{t,i}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}}\right|,\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+UB_{t,i}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}}\right|\right\} |
|
(A.50) |
Since L​Bt,i<vt,i′<U​Bt,iLB_{t,i}<v^{\prime}_{t,i}<UB_{t,i} and the function is decreasing, we have 1/ϵ+U​Bt,i<1/ϵ+vt,i′<1/ϵ+L​Bt,i1/\sqrt{\epsilon+UB_{t,i}}<1/\sqrt{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}<1/\sqrt{\epsilon+LB_{t,i}}. We can therefore remove the absolute value signs:
|
|1ϵ+vt,i−1ϵ+vt,i′|\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v_{t,i}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}}\right| |
≤max⁡{1ϵ+L​Bt,i−1ϵ+vt,i′,1ϵ+vt,i′−1ϵ+U​Bt,i}\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+LB_{t,i}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+UB_{t,i}}}\right\} |
|
(A.51) |
This completes the proof of the second statement.
∎
A.4 Proof of Lemma A.3
Lemma A.3 (Bound on Discrete Error).
Given two discrete-time systems defined for t≥1t\geq 1:
•
System A: at=k⁡(at−1+ct−1)+dta_{t}=k(a_{t-1}+c_{t-1})+d_{t}
•
System B: bt=k​bt−1+dtb_{t}=kb_{t-1}+d_{t}
where the perturbation term ctc_{t} is bounded by |ct|≤q​|at||c_{t}|\leq q|a_{t}| for all tt, and the constants k,qk,q satisfy 0<k<10<k<1 and q<kq<k.
Under zero initial conditions, where a0=b0=0a_{0}=b_{0}=0, the absolute error between the states of the two systems is bounded by:
|
|at−bt|≤∑j=1t−1[(k⁡(1+q))t−j−kt−j]​|dj|\displaystyle|a_{t}-b_{t}|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\left[(k(1+q))^{t-j}-k^{t-j}\right]|d_{j}| |
|
(A.52) |
Proof.
First, define the error as et=at−bte_{t}=a_{t}-b_{t}. Subtracting the two system equations yields the error recurrence relation:
|
et=k​et−1+k​ct−1\displaystyle e_{t}=ke_{t-1}+kc_{t-1} |
|
(A.53) |
The explicit solution to this recurrence is et=kt​e0+∑j=0t−1kt−j​cje_{t}=k^{t}e_{0}+\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}k^{t-j}c_{j}. Under the zero initial condition a0=b0=0a_{0}=b_{0}=0, this simplifies to:
|
et=∑j=0t−1kt−j​cj\displaystyle e_{t}=\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}k^{t-j}c_{j} |
|
(A.54) |
Taking the absolute value and applying the given condition |cj|≤q​|aj||c_{j}|\leq q|a_{j}|, we have:
|
|et|≤∑j=0t−1kt−j​|cj|≤q​∑j=0t−1kt−j​|aj|\displaystyle|e_{t}|\leq\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}k^{t-j}|c_{j}|\leq q\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}k^{t-j}|a_{j}| |
|
(A.55) |
Since a0=0a_{0}=0, the sum starts from j=1j=1. The state |aj||a_{j}| can be bounded from its own recurrence |at|≤k⁡(1+q)​|at−1|+|dt||a_{t}|\leq k(1+q)|a_{t-1}|+|d_{t}|, which for a0=0a_{0}=0 unrolls to:
|
|aj|≤∑i=1j(k⁡(1+q))j−i​|di|\displaystyle|a_{j}|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{j}(k(1+q))^{j-i}|d_{i}| |
|
(A.56) |
Substituting the bound for |aj||a_{j}| into the inequality for |et||e_{t}| gives a double summation:
|
|et|≤q​∑j=1t−1kt−j​(∑i=1j(k⁡(1+q))j−i​|di|)\displaystyle|e_{t}|\leq q\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}k^{t-j}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{j}(k(1+q))^{j-i}|d_{i}|\right) |
|
(A.57) |
By swapping the order of summation and evaluating the inner geometric series, we obtain the final result:
|
|at−bt|≤∑j=1t−1[(k⁡(1+q))t−j−kt−j]​|dj|\displaystyle|a_{t}-b_{t}|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\left[(k(1+q))^{t-j}-k^{t-j}\right]|d_{j}| |
|
(A.58) |
∎
A.5 Proof of Lemma A.4
Lemma A.4 (Finite Geometric Series Ratio Bounded by Infinite Sum).
Let (gk)k=0t(g_{k})_{k=0}^{t} be a sequence of scalars for any finite t∈ℕt\in\mathbb{N}. Let the weights be terms of two geometric series, Ak=akA_{k}=a^{k} and Bk=bkB_{k}=b^{k}, where a,b∈(0,1)a,b\in(0,1) are the base ratios.
If the condition a2<ba^{2}<b holds, then the ratio of the weighted sum is bounded by a constant derived from the corresponding infinite series:
|
∑k=0tak​|gk|∑k=0tbk​gk2≤11−a2/b\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{k=0}^{t}a^{k}|g_{k}|}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{t}b^{k}g_{k}^{2}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{1-a^{2}/b}} |
|
(A.59) |
Proof.
Let the numerator be Nt=∑k=0tak​|gk|N_{t}=\sum_{k=0}^{t}a^{k}|g_{k}| and the denominator be Dt=∑k=0tbk​gk2D_{t}=\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{t}b^{k}g_{k}^{2}}.
We rewrite the numerator as:
|
Nt=∑k=0t(akbk)⋅(bk​|gk|)\displaystyle N_{t}=\sum_{k=0}^{t}\left(\frac{a^{k}}{\sqrt{b^{k}}}\right)\cdot\left(\sqrt{b^{k}}|g_{k}|\right) |
|
(A.60) |
Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to these finite sums, we get:
|
Nt2\displaystyle N_{t}^{2} |
≤(∑k=0t(akbk)2)⋅(∑k=0t(bk​|gk|)2)\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t}\left(\frac{a^{k}}{\sqrt{b^{k}}}\right)^{2}\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t}\left(\sqrt{b^{k}}|g_{k}|\right)^{2}\right) |
|
|
|
=(∑k=0ta2​kbk)⋅(∑k=0tbk​gk2)\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t}\frac{a^{2k}}{b^{k}}\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t}b^{k}g_{k}^{2}\right) |
|
|
|
=(∑k=0t(a2b)k)⋅Dt2\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t}\left(\frac{a^{2}}{b}\right)^{k}\right)\cdot D_{t}^{2} |
|
(A.61) |
The first term is a finite geometric series. Since the condition a2<ba^{2}<b implies that the ratio r=a2/br=a^{2}/b is positive and less than 1, all terms in the series are positive. Therefore, the finite sum is always less than or equal to the sum of the infinite series:
|
∑k=0t(a2b)k≤∑k=0∞(a2b)k=11−a2/b\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{t}\left(\frac{a^{2}}{b}\right)^{k}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{a^{2}}{b}\right)^{k}=\frac{1}{1-a^{2}/b} |
|
(A.62) |
Substituting this upper bound back into the inequality for Nt2N_{t}^{2}, we have:
|
Nt2≤(11−a2/b)⋅Dt2\displaystyle N_{t}^{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{1-a^{2}/b}\right)\cdot D_{t}^{2} |
|
(A.63) |
Taking the square root of both sides gives:
|
Nt≤11−a2/b⋅Dt\displaystyle N_{t}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{1-a^{2}/b}}\cdot D_{t} |
|
(A.64) |
Finally, dividing by DtD_{t} yields the desired result for any finite tt:
|
NtDt=∑k=0tak​|gk|∑k=0tbk​gk2≤11−a2/b\displaystyle\frac{N_{t}}{D_{t}}=\frac{\sum_{k=0}^{t}a^{k}|g_{k}|}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{t}b^{k}g_{k}^{2}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{1-a^{2}/b}} |
|
(A.65) |
∎
A.6 Proof of Lemma A.5
Lemma A.5 (Bound on the Quantized Momentum Error Ratio).
Let (gk)k=0t(g_{k})_{k=0}^{t} be a sequence of scalars. Let the weights be Ak=β1k​((1+qM)k−1)A_{k}=\beta_{1}^{k}((1+q_{M})^{k}-1) and Bk=(β2​(1−qV))kB_{k}=(\beta_{2}(1-q_{V}))^{k}.
If the condition β12​(1+qM)2<β2​(1−qV)\beta_{1}^{2}(1+q_{M})^{2}<\beta_{2}(1-q_{V}) holds, then the ratio of the weighted sum is bounded by:
|
∑k=0tAk​|gk|∑k=0tBk​gk2≤qM⋅r′​(1+r′)(1+qM)​(1−r′)3/2\frac{\sum_{k=0}^{t}A_{k}|g_{k}|}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{t}B_{k}g_{k}^{2}}}\leq q_{M}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{r^{\prime}(1+r^{\prime})}}{(1+q_{M})(1-r^{\prime})^{3/2}} |
|
where r′=β12​(1+qM)2β2​(1−qV)r^{\prime}=\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}(1+q_{M})^{2}}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}.
Proof.
Following the proof of Lemma A.4, we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to get:
|
(∑k=0tAk​|gk|)2≤(∑k=0tAk2Bk)⋅(∑k=0tBk​gk2).\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t}A_{k}|g_{k}|\right)^{2}\leq\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t}\frac{A_{k}^{2}}{B_{k}}\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t}B_{k}g_{k}^{2}\right). |
|
(A.66) |
This implies that the ratio is bounded by the square root of the first term on the right-hand side. We now focus on bounding the term ∑k=0tAk2Bk\sum_{k=0}^{t}\frac{A_{k}^{2}}{B_{k}}. First, we express the ratio Ak2Bk\frac{A_{k}^{2}}{B_{k}} as:
|
Ak2Bk\displaystyle\frac{A_{k}^{2}}{B_{k}} |
=(β1k​((1+qM)k−1))2(β2​(1−qV))k\displaystyle=\frac{(\beta_{1}^{k}((1+q_{M})^{k}-1))^{2}}{(\beta_{2}(1-q_{V}))^{k}} |
|
|
|
=(β12β2​(1−qV))k​((1+qM)k−1)2.\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}\right)^{k}((1+q_{M})^{k}-1)^{2}. |
|
(A.67) |
To bound the term ((1+qM)k−1)2((1+q_{M})^{k}-1)^{2}, we first establish an inequality for (1+qM)k−1(1+q_{M})^{k}-1 using the Mean Value Theorem. Let f⁡(x)=xkf(x)=x^{k}. For qM>0q_{M}>0, by the Mean Value Theorem, there exists a c∈(1,1+qM)c\in(1,1+q_{M}) such that:
|
f⁡(1+qM)−f⁡(1)(1+qM)−1=f′​(c)⟹(1+qM)k−1=qM⋅(k​ck−1).\displaystyle\frac{f(1+q_{M})-f(1)}{(1+q_{M})-1}=f^{\prime}(c)\implies(1+q_{M})^{k}-1=q_{M}\cdot(kc^{k-1}). |
|
(A.68) |
Since c<1+qMc<1+q_{M}, and for k≥1k\geq 1, we have ck−1≤(1+qM)k−1c^{k-1}\leq(1+q_{M})^{k-1}. This leads to the inequality:
|
(1+qM)k−1≤k⋅qM⋅(1+qM)k−1.\displaystyle(1+q_{M})^{k}-1\leq k\cdot q_{M}\cdot(1+q_{M})^{k-1}. |
|
(A.69) |
Squaring both sides of (A.69) gives:
|
((1+qM)k−1)2\displaystyle((1+q_{M})^{k}-1)^{2} |
≤k2​qM2​(1+qM)2​(k−1)\displaystyle\leq k^{2}q_{M}^{2}(1+q_{M})^{2(k-1)} |
|
|
|
=k2​qM2(1+qM)2​(1+qM)2​k.\displaystyle=\frac{k^{2}q_{M}^{2}}{(1+q_{M})^{2}}(1+q_{M})^{2k}. |
|
(A.70) |
Substituting this back into (A.67), and using the definition r′=β12​(1+qM)2β2​(1−qV)r^{\prime}=\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}(1+q_{M})^{2}}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}, we get:
|
Ak2Bk\displaystyle\frac{A_{k}^{2}}{B_{k}} |
≤(β12β2​(1−qV))k​k2​qM2(1+qM)2​(1+qM)2​k\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}\right)^{k}\frac{k^{2}q_{M}^{2}}{(1+q_{M})^{2}}(1+q_{M})^{2k} |
|
|
|
=qM2(1+qM)2​k2​(β12​(1+qM)2β2​(1−qV))k\displaystyle=\frac{q_{M}^{2}}{(1+q_{M})^{2}}k^{2}\left(\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}(1+q_{M})^{2}}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}\right)^{k} |
|
|
|
=qM2(1+qM)2​k2​(r′)k.\displaystyle=\frac{q_{M}^{2}}{(1+q_{M})^{2}}k^{2}(r^{\prime})^{k}. |
|
(A.71) |
Now we sum this term. The condition r′<1r^{\prime}<1 ensures the convergence of the infinite series. We first derive the closed-form expression for ∑k=0∞k2​xk\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}k^{2}x^{k} for |x|<1|x|<1. We start with the geometric series:
|
∑k=0∞xk=11−x.\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}x^{k}=\frac{1}{1-x}. |
|
(A.72) |
Differentiating with respect to xx and multiplying by xx gives:
|
∑k=0∞k​xk=x​dd​x​(11−x)=x(1−x)2.\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}kx^{k}=x\frac{d}{dx}\left(\frac{1}{1-x}\right)=\frac{x}{(1-x)^{2}}. |
|
(A.73) |
Differentiating one more time and multiplying by xx yields:
|
∑k=0∞k2​xk=x​dd​x​(x(1−x)2)=x​1​(1−x)2−x⁡(2​(1−x)​(−1))(1−x)4=x⁡(1+x)(1−x)3.\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}k^{2}x^{k}=x\frac{d}{dx}\left(\frac{x}{(1-x)^{2}}\right)=x\frac{1(1-x)^{2}-x(2(1-x)(-1))}{(1-x)^{4}}=\frac{x(1+x)}{(1-x)^{3}}. |
|
(A.74) |
Using this result with x=r′x=r^{\prime}, we can bound the sum ∑k=0tAk2Bk\sum_{k=0}^{t}\frac{A_{k}^{2}}{B_{k}} by extending it to an infinite series:
|
∑k=0tAk2Bk\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{t}\frac{A_{k}^{2}}{B_{k}} |
≤∑k=0∞Ak2Bk\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{A_{k}^{2}}{B_{k}} |
|
|
|
≤∑k=0∞qM2(1+qM)2​k2​(r′)k\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{q_{M}^{2}}{(1+q_{M})^{2}}k^{2}(r^{\prime})^{k} |
|
|
|
=qM2(1+qM)2​∑k=0∞k2​(r′)k\displaystyle=\frac{q_{M}^{2}}{(1+q_{M})^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}k^{2}(r^{\prime})^{k} |
|
|
|
=qM2(1+qM)2​r′​(1+r′)(1−r′)3.\displaystyle=\frac{q_{M}^{2}}{(1+q_{M})^{2}}\frac{r^{\prime}(1+r^{\prime})}{(1-r^{\prime})^{3}}. |
|
(A.75) |
Finally, taking the square root of (A.75) and substituting it back into the result from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality (A.66) gives the desired bound:
|
∑k=0tAk​|gk|∑k=0tBk​gk2≤∑k=0tAk2Bk≤qM2(1+qM)2​r′​(1+r′)(1−r′)3=qM⋅r′​(1+r′)(1+qM)​(1−r′)3/2.\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{k=0}^{t}A_{k}|g_{k}|}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{t}B_{k}g_{k}^{2}}}\leq\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{t}\frac{A_{k}^{2}}{B_{k}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{q_{M}^{2}}{(1+q_{M})^{2}}\frac{r^{\prime}(1+r^{\prime})}{(1-r^{\prime})^{3}}}=q_{M}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{r^{\prime}(1+r^{\prime})}}{(1+q_{M})(1-r^{\prime})^{3/2}}. |
|
(A.76) |
∎
A.7 Proof of Lemma A.6
Lemma A.6 (Bound on the Quantized Gradient Estimator).
Let the stochastic gradient be bounded in infinity norm almost surely by ‖∇ft​(𝐰,γ)‖∞≤R−ϵ\|\nabla f_{t}(\mathbf{w};\gamma)\|_{\infty}\leq R-\sqrt{\epsilon} for any parameters 𝐰\mathbf{w}. Let the gradient quantization operator satisfy the relative error model |Q⁡(z)−z|≤qG​|z||Q(z)-z|\leq q_{G}|z| for any scalar zz. The quantized gradient estimator 𝐠t^\hat{\mathbf{g}_{t}} is defined component-wise for i∈[d]i\in[d] as:
|
g^t,i=1B​∑j=1B[∇Qf​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)]i,\displaystyle\hat{g}_{t,i}=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{j=1}^{B}[\nabla^{Q}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})]_{i}, |
|
(A.77) |
where we use ∇Qf​(⋅)\nabla^{Q}f(\cdot) as shorthand for Q​(∇f​(⋅))Q(\nabla f(\cdot)) and [⋅]i[\cdot]_{i} to denote the i-th component. Then, the infinity norm of the estimator is bounded almost surely:
|
‖𝐠t^‖∞≤(1+qG)​(R−ϵ).\displaystyle\|\hat{\mathbf{g}_{t}}\|_{\infty}\leq(1+q_{G})(R-\sqrt{\epsilon}). |
|
(A.78) |
For notational simplicity in subsequent proofs, we will use the slightly looser bound ‖𝐠t^‖∞≤(1+qG)​R\|\hat{\mathbf{g}_{t}}\|_{\infty}\leq(1+q_{G})R.
Proof.
We first bound the infinity norm of a single quantized gradient vector ∇Qf​(⋅)\nabla^{Q}f(\cdot). For any component i∈[d]i\in[d], we have:
|
|∇iQ​f​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)|\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{i}^{Q}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})\right| |
=|∇if​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)+(∇iQf​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)−∇if​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j))|\displaystyle=\left|\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})+\left(\nabla_{i}^{Q}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})-\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})\right)\right| |
|
|
|
≤|∇if​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)|+|∇iQf​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)−∇if​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)|\displaystyle\leq\left|\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})\right|+\left|\nabla_{i}^{Q}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})-\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})\right| |
|
|
|
≤|∇if​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)|+qG​|∇if​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)|\displaystyle\leq\left|\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})\right|+q_{G}\left|\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})\right| |
|
|
|
=(1+qG)​|∇if​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)|.\displaystyle=(1+q_{G})\left|\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})\right|. |
|
(A.79) |
Since this holds for any component, it also holds for the component with the maximum absolute value. Therefore, by taking the maximum over i∈[d]i\in[d], we can bound the infinity norm:
|
‖∇Qf​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)‖∞\displaystyle\|\nabla^{Q}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})\|_{\infty} |
≤(1+qG)​‖∇f​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)‖∞\displaystyle\leq(1+q_{G})\|\nabla f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})\|_{\infty} |
|
|
|
≤(1+qG)​(R−ϵ).\displaystyle\leq(1+q_{G})(R-\sqrt{\epsilon}). |
|
(A.80) |
Finally, we apply the triangle inequality to the full estimator 𝐠t^\hat{\mathbf{g}_{t}}, which is the average over BB such vectors:
|
‖𝐠t^‖∞\displaystyle\|\hat{\mathbf{g}_{t}}\|_{\infty} |
=‖1B​∑j=1B∇Qf​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)‖∞\displaystyle=\|\frac{1}{B}\sum_{j=1}^{B}\nabla^{Q}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})\|_{\infty} |
|
|
|
≤1B​∑j=1B‖∇Qf​(𝐰t−1Q,γt,j)‖∞\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{B}\sum_{j=1}^{B}\|\nabla^{Q}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma_{t,j})\|_{\infty} |
|
|
|
≤1B​∑j=1B(1+qG)​(R−ϵ)\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{B}\sum_{j=1}^{B}(1+q_{G})(R-\sqrt{\epsilon}) |
|
|
|
=(1+qG)​(R−ϵ).\displaystyle=(1+q_{G})(R-\sqrt{\epsilon}). |
|
(A.81) |
This concludes the proof.
∎
A.8 Proof of Lemma A.7
Lemma A.7 (Bound on the Expected Gradient Error with Biased Quantization).
Under the assumptions that the infinity norm of stochastic gradient is up bounded (Assumption 4.2), the objective FF is L-smooth (Assumption 4.3), and the quantization relative error model holds (Assumption 3.1), the magnitude of the conditional expectation of the total error term δt,i\delta_{t,i} is bounded by:
|
|𝔼t−1​[δt,i]|≤qG​R+L​qW​‖𝐰t−1‖2.|\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\left[\delta_{t,i}\right]|\leq q_{G}R+Lq_{W}\|\mathbf{w}_{t-1}\|_{2}. |
|
Proof.
We start from the decomposition of the conditional expectation of δt,i\delta_{t,i}, which we derived previously:
|
𝔼t−1​[δt,i]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\left[\delta_{t,i}\right] |
=𝔼γ​[∇iQf​(𝐰t−1Q,γ)−∇if​(𝐰t−1Q,γ)]+𝔼γ​[∇if​(𝐰t−1Q,γ)−∇if​(𝐰t−1,γ)]\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\gamma}\left[\nabla_{i}^{Q}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma)-\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma)\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\gamma}\left[\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma)-\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1};\gamma)\right] |
|
|
|
=𝔼γ​[∇iQf​(𝐰t−1Q,γ)−∇if​(𝐰t−1Q,γ)]⏟Term I: Gradient Quantization Bias+(∇iF​(𝐰t−1Q)−∇iF​(𝐰t−1))⏟Term II: Weight Quantization Bias\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\gamma}\left[\nabla_{i}^{Q}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma)-\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma)\right]}_{\text{Term I: Gradient Quantization Bias}}+\underbrace{\left(\nabla_{i}F(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q})-\nabla_{i}F(\mathbf{w}_{t-1})\right)}_{\text{Term II: Weight Quantization Bias}} |
|
(A.82) |
Using the triangle inequality, we can bound the magnitude as:
|
|𝔼t−1​[δt,i]|≤|Term I|+|Term II|.\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\left[\delta_{t,i}\right]|\leq|\text{Term I}|+|\text{Term II}|. |
|
(A.83) |
We bound each term separately.
Bounding Term I:
This term is the expected bias from the (potentially biased) gradient quantization. We first apply Jensen’s inequality for absolute values, i.e., |𝔼⁡[X]|≤𝔼⁡[|X|]|\mathbb{E}[X]|\leq\mathbb{E}[|X|]:
|
|Term I|\displaystyle|\text{Term I}| |
=|𝔼γ​[∇iQf​(𝐰t−1Q,γ)−∇if​(𝐰t−1Q,γ)]|\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}_{\gamma}\left[\nabla_{i}^{Q}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma)-\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma)\right]\right| |
|
|
|
≤𝔼γ​[|∇iQf​(𝐰t−1Q,γ)−∇if​(𝐰t−1Q,γ)|].\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\gamma}\left[\left|\nabla_{i}^{Q}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma)-\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma)\right|\right]. |
|
(A.84) |
By the relative error model for gradient quantization (Assumption 3.1 with factor qGq_{G}):
|
|Term I|≤𝔼γ​[qG​|∇if​(𝐰t−1Q,γ)|]≤qG​R.\displaystyle|\text{Term I}|\leq\mathbb{E}_{\gamma}\left[q_{G}\left|\nabla_{i}f(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q};\gamma)\right|\right]\leq q_{G}R. |
|
(A.85) |
Bounding Term II:
This term represents the bias from weight quantization. Using the L-smoothness of FF (Assumption 4.3) and the relative error for weights (Assumption 3.1):
|
|Term II|=|∇iF​(𝐰t−1Q)−∇iF​(𝐰t−1)|≤‖∇F​(𝐰t−1Q)−∇F​(𝐰t−1)‖2≤L​qW​‖𝐰t−1‖2.\displaystyle|\text{Term II}|=|\nabla_{i}F(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q})-\nabla_{i}F(\mathbf{w}_{t-1})|\leq\|\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{Q})-\nabla F(\mathbf{w}_{t-1})\|_{2}\leq Lq_{W}\|\mathbf{w}_{t-1}\|_{2}. |
|
(A.86) |
Combining the Bounds:
Summing the bounds for Term I and Term II, we arrive at the final result:
|
|𝔼t−1​[δt,i]|≤|Term I|+|Term II|≤qG​R+L​qW​‖𝐰t−1‖2.\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\left[\delta_{t,i}\right]|\leq|\text{Term I}|+|\text{Term II}|\leq q_{G}R+Lq_{W}\|\mathbf{w}_{t-1}\|_{2}. |
|
(A.87) |
∎
A.9 Proof of Lemma A.8 (Bound on Term D)
Lemma A.8 (Bound on Term D).
The term D, which captures the error from gradient drift as defined in (A.20), is bounded by:
|
|D|≤ηt2​L2​1−β14​(1+qG)​R​(∑l=1t−1‖𝐮t−l‖22​∑k=lt−1β1k​k)+(1+qG)​R1−β1​∑k=0t−1(β1β2)k​k+1​‖𝐔t−k‖22.\displaystyle|D|\leq\frac{\eta_{t}^{2}L^{2}\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}{4(1+q_{G})R}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{t-1}\|\mathbf{u}_{t-l}\|_{2}^{2}\sum_{k=l}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}\sqrt{k}\right)+\frac{(1+q_{G})R}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\left(\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{2}}\right)^{k}\sqrt{k+1}\|\mathbf{U}_{t-k}\|_{2}^{2}. |
|
(A.88) |
Proof.
We start with the definition of Term D:
|
D=∑i∈[d]∑k=0t−1β1k​(𝒢t,i−𝒢t−k,i)​gt−k,i+δt−k,iϵ+vt,i′D=\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}\left(\mathcal{G}_{t,i}-\mathcal{G}_{t-k,i}\right)\frac{g_{t-k,i}+\delta_{t-k,i}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}} |
|
To tackle this, we employ the weighted Young’s inequality, which states that for any λ>0\lambda>0,
|
x​y≤λ2​x2+12​λ​y2xy\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}x^{2}+\frac{1}{2\lambda}y^{2} |
|
(A.89) |
We apply this inequality to each product within the summation for Term D, setting
|
x=|𝒢t,i−𝒢t−k,i|,y=|gt−k,i+δt−k,i|ϵ+vt,i′,andλ=1−β12​(1+qG)​R​k+1.x=|{\mathcal{G}_{t,i}-\mathcal{G}_{t-k,i}}|,\quad y=\frac{|{g_{t-k,i}+\delta_{t-k,i}}|}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}},\quad\text{and}\quad\lambda=\frac{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}{2(1+q_{G})R\sqrt{k+1}}. |
|
This application gives us an initial bound on the magnitude of D:
|
|D|\displaystyle|D| |
≤∑i∈[d]∑k=0t−1β1k​(1−β14​(1+qG)​R​k+1​(𝒢t,i−𝒢t−k,i)2+(1+qG)​R​k+11−β1​(gt−k,i+δt−k,i)2ϵ+vt,i′).\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}\left(\frac{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}{4(1+q_{G})R\sqrt{k+1}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{t,i}-\mathcal{G}_{t-k,i}\right)^{2}+\frac{(1+q_{G})R\sqrt{k+1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}\frac{(g_{t-k,i}+\delta_{t-k,i})^{2}}{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}\right). |
|
(A.90) |
To simplify this expression further, we must establish bounds for two of its key components.
First, we can find a lower bound for the denominator term. For any coordinate i∈[d]i\in[d], the recursive definition of vt,i′v^{\prime}_{t,i} implies that ϵ+vt,i′≥ϵ+β2k​vt−k,i′≥β2k​(ϵ+vt−k,i′)\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}\geq\epsilon+\beta_{2}^{k}v^{\prime}_{t-k,i}\geq\beta_{2}^{k}(\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t-k,i}). This allows us to bound the fraction as:
|
(gt−k,i+δt−k,i)2ϵ+vt,i′≤1β2k​Ut−k,i2.\frac{(g_{t-k,i}+\delta_{t-k,i})^{2}}{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}\leq\frac{1}{\beta_{2}^{k}}U_{t-k,i}^{2}. |
|
(A.91) |
Second, we bound the squared gradient difference using the L-smoothness of the objective function FF.
|
‖𝒢t−𝒢t−k‖22\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}_{t}-\mathcal{G}_{t-k}\|_{2}^{2} |
≤L2​‖𝐰t−1−𝐰t−k−1‖22=L2​‖∑l=1kηt−l​𝐮t−l‖22\displaystyle\leq L^{2}\|\mathbf{w}_{t-1}-\mathbf{w}_{t-k-1}\|_{2}^{2}=L^{2}\left\|\sum_{l=1}^{k}\eta_{t-l}\mathbf{u}_{t-l}\right\|_{2}^{2} |
|
|
|
≤ηt2​L2​k​∑l=1k‖𝐮t−l‖22.\displaystyle\leq\eta_{t}^{2}L^{2}k\sum_{l=1}^{k}\|\mathbf{u}_{t-l}\|^{2}_{2}. |
|
(A.92) |
The final step above follows from Jensen’s inequality and the fact that the step size schedule ηt\eta_{t} is non-decreasing.
With these two intermediate results, (A.91) and (A.92), we can return to our main inequality (A.90). Substituting these bounds yields:
|
|D|\displaystyle|D| |
≤(∑k=0t−1ηt2​L2​1−β1​β1k4​(1+qG)​R​k+1​(k​∑l=1k‖𝐮t−l‖22))+(∑k=0t−1(1+qG)​R​β1k​k+11−β1​β2k​‖𝐔t−k‖22)\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\frac{\eta_{t}^{2}L^{2}\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}\beta_{1}^{k}}{4(1+q_{G})R\sqrt{k+1}}\left(k\sum_{l=1}^{k}\|\mathbf{u}_{t-l}\|^{2}_{2}\right)\right)+\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\frac{(1+q_{G})R\beta_{1}^{k}\sqrt{k+1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}\beta_{2}^{k}}\|\mathbf{U}_{t-k}\|_{2}^{2}\right) |
|
|
|
≤ηt2​L2​1−β14​(1+qG)​R​(∑k=0t−1β1k​k​∑l=1k‖𝐮t−l‖22)+(1+qG)​R1−β1​∑k=0t−1(β1β2)k​k+1​‖𝐔t−k‖22.\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta_{t}^{2}L^{2}\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}{4(1+q_{G})R}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}\sqrt{k}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\|\mathbf{u}_{t-l}\|^{2}_{2}\right)+\frac{(1+q_{G})R}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\left(\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{2}}\right)^{k}\sqrt{k+1}\|\mathbf{U}_{t-k}\|_{2}^{2}. |
|
Finally, by rearranging the order of summation in the first term, we arrive at our desired bound:
|
|D|≤ηt2​L2​1−β14​(1+qG)​R​(∑l=1t−1‖𝐮t−l‖22​∑k=lt−1β1k​k)+(1+qG)​R1−β1​∑k=0t−1(β1β2)k​k+1​‖𝐔t−k‖22.\displaystyle|D|\leq\frac{\eta_{t}^{2}L^{2}\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}{4(1+q_{G})R}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{t-1}\|\mathbf{u}_{t-l}\|_{2}^{2}\sum_{k=l}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}\sqrt{k}\right)+\frac{(1+q_{G})R}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\left(\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{2}}\right)^{k}\sqrt{k+1}\|\mathbf{U}_{t-k}\|_{2}^{2}. |
|
(A.93) |
∎
A.10 Proof of Lemma A.9 (Lower Bound on Term C)
Lemma A.9 (Lower Bound on the Expectation of Term C).
The expectation of term C, defined in (A.20), is lower-bounded by:
|
𝔼⁡[C]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[C\right] |
≥12​(∑i∈[d]∑k=0t−1β1k​𝔼​[𝒢t−k,i2ϵ+v~t,k+1,i])−2​(1+qG)​R1−β1​(∑i∈[d]∑k=0t−1(β1β2)k​k+1​𝔼​[‖𝐔t−k‖22])\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathcal{G}_{t-k,i}^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}_{t,k+1,i}}}\right]\right)-\frac{2(1+q_{G})R}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}\left(\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\left(\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{2}}\right)^{k}\sqrt{k+1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{U}_{t-k}\|_{2}^{2}\right]\right) |
|
|
|
−d∑k=0t−1β1kMt−k.\displaystyle\quad-d\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}M_{t-k}. |
|
(A.94) |
where Mt−k=qG​R2+L​qW​R​‖𝐰t−k−1‖2ϵM_{t-k}=\frac{q_{G}R^{2}+Lq_{W}R\|\mathbf{w}_{t-k-1}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}.
Proof.
We study the main term of the summation in C, i.e. for i∈[d]i\in[d] and k<tk<t:
|
𝔼⁡[𝒢t−k,i​gt−k,i+δt−k,iϵ+vt,i]=𝔼⁡[∇iF​(𝐰t−k−1)​∇ift−k​(𝐰t−k−1)+δt−k,iϵ+vt,i].\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{G}_{t-k,i}\frac{g_{t-k,i}+\delta_{t-k,i}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v_{t,i}}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{i}F(\mathbf{w}_{t-k-1})\frac{\nabla_{i}f_{t-k}(\mathbf{w}_{t-k-1})+\delta_{t-k,i}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v_{t,i}}}\right]. |
|
(A.95) |
We will further drop indices in the rest of the proof, noting 𝒢=𝒢t−k,i\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}_{t-k,i},
g=gt−k,ig=g_{t-k,i}, δ=δt−k,i\delta=\delta_{t-k,i},v~=v~t,k+1,i\tilde{v}=\tilde{v}_{t,k+1,i} and v=vt,i′v=v^{\prime}_{t,i}. Finally, let us note
|
s2=∑j=t−ktβ2t−j​(gj,i+δj,i)2andr2=𝔼t−k−1​[s2].s^{2}=\sum_{j=t-k}^{t}\beta_{2}^{t-j}(g_{j,i}+\delta_{j,i})^{2}\qquad\text{and}\qquad r^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{t-k-1}\left[s^{2}\right]. |
|
(A.96) |
In particular we have v~−v=r2−s2\tilde{v}-v=r^{2}-s^{2}. With our new notations, we can rewrite (A.95) as
|
𝔼⁡[𝒢​g+δϵ+v]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{G}\frac{g+\delta}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v}}\right] |
=𝔼⁡[𝒢​g+δϵ+v~+𝒢⁡(g+δ)​(1ϵ+v−1ϵ+v~)]\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{G}\frac{g+\delta}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}+\mathcal{G}(g+\delta)\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}\right)\right] |
|
|
|
=𝔼⁡[𝔼t−k−1​[𝒢​g+δϵ+v~]+𝒢⁡(g+δ)​r2−s2ϵ+v​ϵ+v~​(ϵ+v+ϵ+v~)]\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}_{t-k-1}\left[\mathcal{G}\frac{g+\delta}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}\right]+\mathcal{G}(g+\delta)\frac{r^{2}-s^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v}\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}(\sqrt{\epsilon+v}+\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}})}\right] |
|
|
|
=𝔼⁡[𝒢2ϵ+v~]+𝔼⁡[𝒢​𝔼t−k−1​[δ]ϵ+v~]+𝔼⁡[𝒢⁡(g+δ)​r2−s2ϵ+v​ϵ+v~​(ϵ+v+ϵ+v~)⏟E]\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathcal{G}^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathcal{G}\mathbb{E}_{t-k-1}\left[\delta\right]}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\underbrace{\mathcal{G}(g+\delta)\frac{r^{2}-s^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v}\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}(\sqrt{\epsilon+v}+\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}})}}_{E}\right] |
|
|
|
≥𝔼⁡[𝒢2ϵ+v~]−qG​R2+L​qW​R​‖𝐰t−k−1‖2ϵ+𝔼⁡[E].\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathcal{G}^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}\right]-\frac{q_{G}R^{2}+Lq_{W}R\|\mathbf{w}_{t-k-1}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}+\mathbb{E}\left[E\right]. |
|
(A.97) |
The inequality uses Lemma A.7 and the bound for ‖∇F​(⋅)‖∞\|\nabla F(\cdot)\|_{\infty}. We denote qG​R2+L​qW​R​‖𝐰t−k−1‖2ϵ≜Mt−k\frac{q_{G}R^{2}+Lq_{W}R\|\mathbf{w}_{t-k-1}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\triangleq M_{t-k}.
Then we focus on EE:
|
|E|\displaystyle|E| |
≤|𝒢⁡(g+δ)|​r2ϵ+v​(ϵ+v~)⏟κ+|𝒢⁡(g+δ)|​s2(ϵ+v)​ϵ+v~⏟ρ,\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{|\mathcal{G}(g+\delta)|\frac{r^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v}(\epsilon+\tilde{v})}}_{\kappa}+\underbrace{|\mathcal{G}(g+\delta)|\frac{s^{2}}{(\epsilon+v)\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}}_{\rho}, |
|
due to the fact that ϵ+v+ϵ+v~≥max⁡(ϵ+v,ϵ+v~)\sqrt{\epsilon+v}+\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}\geq\max(\sqrt{\epsilon+v},\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}) and
|r2−s2|≤r2+s2|r^{2}-s^{2}|\leq r^{2}+s^{2}.
Applying Young’s inequality to κ\kappa with
|
λ=1−β1​ϵ+v~2,x=|𝒢|ϵ+v~,y=|g+δ|​r2ϵ+v~​ϵ+v,\lambda=\frac{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}{2},\;x=\frac{|\mathcal{G}|}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}},\;y=\frac{|g+\delta|r^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}\sqrt{\epsilon+v}}, |
|
we obtain
|
κ≤𝒢24​ϵ+v~+11−β1​(g+δ)2​r4(ϵ+v~)3/2​(ϵ+v).\displaystyle\kappa\leq\frac{\mathcal{G}^{2}}{4\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}\frac{(g+\delta)^{2}r^{4}}{(\epsilon+\tilde{v})^{3/2}(\epsilon+v)}. |
|
Given that ϵ+v~≥r2\epsilon+\tilde{v}\geq r^{2} and taking the conditional expectation, we can simplify as
|
𝔼t−k−1​[κ]≤𝒢24​ϵ+v~+11−β1​r2ϵ+v~​𝔼t−k−1​[(g+δ)2ϵ+v].\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-k-1}\left[\kappa\right]\leq\frac{\mathcal{G}^{2}}{4\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}\frac{r^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}\mathbb{E}_{t-k-1}\left[\frac{(g+\delta)^{2}}{\epsilon+v}\right]. |
|
(A.98) |
Now turning to ρ\rho, we use Young’s inequality with
|
λ=1−β1​ϵ+v~2​r2,x=|𝒢​s|ϵ+v~,y=|s⁡(g+δ)|ϵ+v,\lambda=\frac{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}{2r^{2}},\;x=\frac{|\mathcal{G}s|}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}},\;y=\frac{|s(g+\delta)|}{\epsilon+v}, |
|
we obtain
|
ρ≤𝒢24​ϵ+v~​s2r2+11−β1​r2ϵ+v~​(g+δ)2​s2(ϵ+v)2.\displaystyle\rho\leq\frac{\mathcal{G}^{2}}{4\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}\frac{s^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}\frac{r^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}\frac{(g+\delta)^{2}s^{2}}{(\epsilon+v)^{2}}. |
|
(A.99) |
Given that ϵ+v≥s2\epsilon+v\geq s^{2}, and 𝔼t−k−1​[s2r2]=1\mathbb{E}_{t-k-1}\left[\frac{s^{2}}{r^{2}}\right]=1, we obtain after taking the conditional expectation,
|
𝔼t−k−1​[ρ]≤𝒢24​ϵ+v~+11−β1​r2ϵ+v~​𝔼t−k−1​[(g+δ)2ϵ+v].\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-k-1}\left[\rho\right]\leq\frac{\mathcal{G}^{2}}{4\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}\frac{r^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}\mathbb{E}_{t-k-1}\left[\frac{(g+\delta)^{2}}{\epsilon+v}\right]. |
|
(A.100) |
Notice that in (A.99), we possibly divide by zero. It suffice to notice that if r2=0r^{2}=0 then s2=0s^{2}=0 a.s. so that ρ=0\rho=0 and (A.100) is still verified.
Summing (A.98) and (A.100), we get
|
𝔼t−k−1​[|E|]≤𝒢22​ϵ+v~+21−β1​r2ϵ+v~​𝔼t−k−1​[(g+δ)2ϵ+v].\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-k-1}\left[|E|\right]\leq\frac{\mathcal{G}^{2}}{2\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}\frac{r^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}}}\mathbb{E}_{t-k-1}\left[\frac{(g+\delta)^{2}}{\epsilon+v}\right]. |
|
(A.101) |
Given that r≤ϵ+v~r\leq\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}} by definition of v~\tilde{v}, and that r≤k+1​(1+qG)​Rr\leq\sqrt{k+1}(1+q_{G})R, reintroducing the indices we had dropped
|
𝔼t−k−1​[|E|]≤𝒢t−k,i22​ϵ+v~t,k+1,i+2​(1+qG)​R1−β1​k+1​𝔼t−k−1​[(gt−k,i+δt−k,i)2ϵ+vt,i′].\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-k-1}\left[|E|\right]\leq\frac{\mathcal{G}_{t-k,i}^{2}}{2\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}_{t,k+1,i}}}+\frac{2(1+q_{G})R}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}\sqrt{k+1}\mathbb{E}_{t-k-1}\left[\frac{(g_{t-k,i}+\delta_{t-k,i})^{2}}{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}}\right]. |
|
(A.102) |
Taking the complete expectation and using that by definition ϵ+vt,i′≥ϵ+β2k​vt−k,i′≥β2k​(ϵ+vt−k,i′)\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t,i}\geq\epsilon+\beta_{2}^{k}v^{\prime}_{t-k,i}\geq\beta_{2}^{k}(\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t-k,i}) we get
|
𝔼⁡[|E|]≤12​𝔼​[𝒢t−k,i2ϵ+v~t,k+1,i]+2​(1+qG)​R1−β1​β2k​k+1​𝔼​[(gt−k,i+δt−k,i)2ϵ+vt−k,i′].\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|E|\right]\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathcal{G}_{t-k,i}^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}_{t,k+1,i}}}\right]+\frac{2(1+q_{G})R}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}\beta_{2}^{k}}\sqrt{k+1}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(g_{t-k,i}+\delta_{t-k,i})^{2}}{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t-k,i}}\right]. |
|
(A.103) |
Injecting (A.103) into (A.97) gives us
|
|
∑i∈[d]∑k=0t−1β1k​𝔼​[𝒢t−k,i​gt−k,i+δt−k,iϵ+vt,i]\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{G}_{t-k,i}\frac{g_{t-k,i}+\delta_{t-k,i}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+v_{t,i}}}\right] |
|
|
|
≥∑i∈[d]∑k=0t−1β1k​(𝔼⁡[𝒢t−k,i2ϵ+v~t,k+1,i]−𝔼⁡[|E|]−Mt−k)\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathcal{G}_{t-k,i}^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}_{t,k+1,i}}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[|E|\right]-M_{t-k}\right) |
|
(A.104) |
|
|
≥∑i∈[d]∑k=0t−1β1k​(𝔼⁡[𝒢t−k,i2ϵ+v~t,k+1,i]−(12​𝔼​[𝒢t−k,i2ϵ+v~t,k,i]+2​(1+qG)​R​k+11−β1​β2k​𝔼​[(gt−k,i+δt−k,i)2ϵ+vt−k,i′]+Mt−k))\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathcal{G}_{t-k,i}^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}_{t,k+1,i}}}\right]-\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathcal{G}_{t-k,i}^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}_{t,k,i}}}\right]+\frac{2(1+q_{G})R\sqrt{k+1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}\beta_{2}^{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(g_{t-k,i}+\delta_{t-k,i})^{2}}{\epsilon+v^{\prime}_{t-k,i}}\right]+M_{t-k}\right)\right) |
|
|
|
≥12​(∑i∈[d]∑k=0t−1β1k​𝔼​[𝒢t−k,i2ϵ+v~t,k+1,i])−2​(1+qG)​R1−β1​(∑i∈[d]∑k=0t−1(β1β2)k​k+1​𝔼​[‖𝐔t−k‖22])−d​∑k=0t−1β1k​Mt−k.\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathcal{G}_{t-k,i}^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon+\tilde{v}_{t,k+1,i}}}\right]\right)-\frac{2(1+q_{G})R}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}}}\left(\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\left(\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{2}}\right)^{k}\sqrt{k+1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{U}_{t-k}\|_{2}^{2}\right]\right)-d\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}M_{t-k}. |
|
(A.105) |
This is the desired lower bound for 𝔼⁡[C]\mathbb{E}\left[C\right].
∎
A.11 Proof of Lemma A.10
Lemma A.10 (Lemma A.2 in Défossez et al. [2022]).
Assume we have 0<β1<β2≤10<\beta_{1}<\beta_{2}\leq 1 and a sequence of real numbers (an)n∈ℕ∗(a_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}. We define for all n∈ℕ∗n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}:
|
bn=∑j=1nβ2n−j​aj2andcn=∑j=1nβ1n−j​aj.b_{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\beta_{2}^{n-j}a_{j}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad c_{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\beta_{1}^{n-j}a_{j}. |
|
Then for any ϵ>0\epsilon>0, we have the following inequality:
|
∑j=1ncj2ϵ+bj≤1(1−β1)​(1−β1/β2)​(ln⁡(1+bnϵ)−n​ln⁡(β2)).\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{c_{j}^{2}}{\epsilon+b_{j}}\leq\frac{1}{(1-\beta_{1})(1-\beta_{1}/\beta_{2})}\left(\ln\left(1+\frac{b_{n}}{\epsilon}\right)-n\ln(\beta_{2})\right). |
|
(A.106) |
Proof.
First, we use Jensen’s inequality on cj2c_{j}^{2}, noting that ∑l=1jβ1j−l=1−β1j1−β1≤11−β1\sum_{l=1}^{j}\beta_{1}^{j-l}=\frac{1-\beta_{1}^{j}}{1-\beta_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}, to get:
|
cj2=(∑l=1jβ1j−l​al)2≤(∑l=1jβ1j−l)​(∑l=1jβ1j−l​al2)≤11−β1​∑l=1jβ1j−l​al2.\displaystyle c_{j}^{2}=\left(\sum_{l=1}^{j}\beta_{1}^{j-l}a_{l}\right)^{2}\leq\left(\sum_{l=1}^{j}\beta_{1}^{j-l}\right)\left(\sum_{l=1}^{j}\beta_{1}^{j-l}a_{l}^{2}\right)\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\sum_{l=1}^{j}\beta_{1}^{j-l}a_{l}^{2}. |
|
Dividing by ϵ+bj\epsilon+b_{j} and using the fact that for l≤jl\leq j, bj≥β2j−l​blb_{j}\geq\beta_{2}^{j-l}b_{l}, which implies ϵ+bj≥β2j−l​(ϵ+bl)\epsilon+b_{j}\geq\beta_{2}^{j-l}(\epsilon+b_{l}), we obtain:
|
cj2ϵ+bj≤11−β1​∑l=1jβ1j−l​al2ϵ+bj≤11−β1​∑l=1j(β1β2)j−l​al2ϵ+bl.\displaystyle\frac{c_{j}^{2}}{\epsilon+b_{j}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\sum_{l=1}^{j}\beta_{1}^{j-l}\frac{a_{l}^{2}}{\epsilon+b_{j}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\sum_{l=1}^{j}\left(\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{2}}\right)^{j-l}\frac{a_{l}^{2}}{\epsilon+b_{l}}. |
|
(A.107) |
Now, we sum over j∈[n]j\in[n] and swap the order of summation:
|
∑j=1ncj2ϵ+bj\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{c_{j}^{2}}{\epsilon+b_{j}} |
≤11−β1​∑j=1n∑l=1j(β1β2)j−l​al2ϵ+bl=11−β1​∑l=1nal2ϵ+bl​∑j=ln(β1β2)j−l\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{j}\left(\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{2}}\right)^{j-l}\frac{a_{l}^{2}}{\epsilon+b_{l}}=\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\frac{a_{l}^{2}}{\epsilon+b_{l}}\sum_{j=l}^{n}\left(\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{2}}\right)^{j-l} |
|
|
|
≤1(1−β1)​(1−β1/β2)​∑l=1nal2ϵ+bl,\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\beta_{1})(1-\beta_{1}/\beta_{2})}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\frac{a_{l}^{2}}{\epsilon+b_{l}}, |
|
(A.108) |
where the last step uses the sum of a geometric series, since β1/β2<1\beta_{1}/\beta_{2}<1.
The next step is to bound the final sum. Let’s denote xl=al2x_{l}=a_{l}^{2}. The sum is ∑l=1nxlϵ+bl\sum_{l=1}^{n}\frac{x_{l}}{\epsilon+b_{l}}, where bl=∑k=1lβ2l−k​xkb_{l}=\sum_{k=1}^{l}\beta_{2}^{l-k}x_{k}. Note that bl−xl=β2​bl−1b_{l}-x_{l}=\beta_{2}b_{l-1} (with b0=0b_{0}=0). Using the inequality xy≤ln⁡(y)−ln⁡(y−x)\frac{x}{y}\leq\ln(y)-\ln(y-x) for 0<x<y0<x<y, we have:
|
xlϵ+bl\displaystyle\frac{x_{l}}{\epsilon+b_{l}} |
≤ln⁡(ϵ+bl)−ln⁡(ϵ+bl−xl)\displaystyle\leq\ln(\epsilon+b_{l})-\ln(\epsilon+b_{l}-x_{l}) |
|
|
|
=ln⁡(ϵ+bl)−ln⁡(ϵ+β2​bl−1)\displaystyle=\ln(\epsilon+b_{l})-\ln(\epsilon+\beta_{2}b_{l-1}) |
|
|
|
=ln⁡(ϵ+blϵ+bl−1)+ln⁡(ϵ+bl−1ϵ+β2​bl−1).\displaystyle=\ln\left(\frac{\epsilon+b_{l}}{\epsilon+b_{l-1}}\right)+\ln\left(\frac{\epsilon+b_{l-1}}{\epsilon+\beta_{2}b_{l-1}}\right). |
|
Summing from l=1l=1 to nn, the first term forms a telescoping series equal to ln⁡(ϵ+bn)−ln⁡(ϵ)=ln⁡(1+bn/ϵ)\ln(\epsilon+b_{n})-\ln(\epsilon)=\ln(1+b_{n}/\epsilon). For the second term, since β2≤1\beta_{2}\leq 1 and bl−1≥0b_{l-1}\geq 0, we have ϵ+β2​bl−1ϵ+bl−1≥β2\frac{\epsilon+\beta_{2}b_{l-1}}{\epsilon+b_{l-1}}\geq\beta_{2}, which implies ln⁡(ϵ+bl−1ϵ+β2​bl−1)≤−ln⁡(β2)\ln\left(\frac{\epsilon+b_{l-1}}{\epsilon+\beta_{2}b_{l-1}}\right)\leq-\ln(\beta_{2}). Thus, summing over ll gives:
|
∑l=1nal2ϵ+bl≤ln⁡(1+bnϵ)−n​ln⁡(β2).\sum_{l=1}^{n}\frac{a_{l}^{2}}{\epsilon+b_{l}}\leq\ln\left(1+\frac{b_{n}}{\epsilon}\right)-n\ln(\beta_{2}). |
|
(A.109) |
This inequality is a useful result in itself, corresponding to the special case where cj2c_{j}^{2} is replaced by aj2a_{j}^{2} (or equivalently β1→0\beta_{1}\to 0 and aja_{j} is replaced by aj2a_{j}^{2}).
Finally, substituting the bound from (A.109) into (A.108) yields the desired result.
∎
A.12 Proof of Lemma A.11
Lemma A.11 (Lemma A.3 in Défossez et al. [2022]).
For any scalar ρ∈(0,1)\rho\in(0,1) and any integer K∈ℕK\in\mathbb{N}, the following bound holds for the finite geometric sum:
|
∑k=0K−1ρk​k+1≤2(1−ρ)3/2.\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\rho^{k}\sqrt{k+1}\leq\frac{2}{(1-\rho)^{3/2}}. |
|
(A.110) |
Proof.
Let the sum be denoted by SK=∑k=0K−1ρk​k+1S_{K}=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\rho^{k}\sqrt{k+1}. We analyze the term (1−ρ)​SK(1-\rho)S_{K}:
|
(1−ρ)​SK\displaystyle(1-\rho)S_{K} |
=∑k=0K−1ρk​k+1−∑j=1Kρj​j\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\rho^{k}\sqrt{k+1}-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\rho^{j}\sqrt{j} |
|
|
|
=1+∑k=1K−1ρk​(k+1−k)−ρK​K.\displaystyle=1+\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\rho^{k}(\sqrt{k+1}-\sqrt{k})-\rho^{K}\sqrt{K}. |
|
By the concavity of the square root function, k+1−k≤12​k\sqrt{k+1}-\sqrt{k}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{k}}. This implies:
|
(1−ρ)​SK\displaystyle(1-\rho)S_{K} |
≤1+∑k=1K−1ρk2​k≤1+∫0∞ρt2​t​𝑑t.\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\frac{\rho^{k}}{2\sqrt{k}}\leq 1+\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\rho^{t}}{2\sqrt{t}}\mathrm{d}t. |
|
The integral is a standard Gaussian integral form which evaluates to π2​−ln⁡(ρ)\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2\sqrt{-\ln(\rho)}}. Using the inequality −ln⁡(ρ)≥1−ρ-\ln(\rho)\geq 1-\rho, we have:
|
(1−ρ)​SK≤1+π2​1−ρ≤21−ρ.\displaystyle(1-\rho)S_{K}\leq 1+\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2\sqrt{1-\rho}}\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{1-\rho}}. |
|
Dividing by (1−ρ)(1-\rho) yields the desired result.
∎
A.13 Proof of Lemma A.12
Lemma A.12 (Lemma A.4 in Défossez et al. [2022]).
For any scalar ρ∈(0,1)\rho\in(0,1) and any integer K∈ℕK\in\mathbb{N}, the following bound holds:
|
∑k=0K−1ρk​k​(k+1)≤4​ρ(1−ρ)5/2.\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\rho^{k}\sqrt{k}(k+1)\leq\frac{4\rho}{(1-\rho)^{5/2}}. |
|
(A.111) |
Proof.
Let the sum be denoted by SK=∑k=0K−1ρk​k​(k+1)S_{K}=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\rho^{k}\sqrt{k}(k+1). We proceed by analyzing (1−ρ)​SK(1-\rho)S_{K}:
|
(1−ρ)​SK\displaystyle(1-\rho)S_{K} |
=∑k=1K−1ρk​[k​(k+1)−k​k−1]−ρK​k​K−1\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\rho^{k}\left[\sqrt{k}(k+1)-k\sqrt{k-1}\right]-\rho^{K}k\sqrt{K-1} |
|
|
|
≤∑k=1K−1ρk​(2​k),\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\rho^{k}(2\sqrt{k}), |
|
where the inequality holds because k​(k+1)−k​k−1≤2​k\sqrt{k}(k+1)-k\sqrt{k-1}\leq 2\sqrt{k}. Re-indexing the sum gives:
|
(1−ρ)​SK\displaystyle(1-\rho)S_{K} |
≤2​ρ​∑k=1K−1ρk−1​k=2​ρ​∑j=0K−2ρj​j+1.\displaystyle\leq 2\rho\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\rho^{k-1}\sqrt{k}=2\rho\sum_{j=0}^{K-2}\rho^{j}\sqrt{j+1}. |
|
Applying the result from Lemma A.11 to the final sum, we get:
|
(1−ρ)​SK≤2​ρ​(2(1−ρ)3/2)=4​ρ(1−ρ)3/2.\displaystyle(1-\rho)S_{K}\leq 2\rho\left(\frac{2}{(1-\rho)^{3/2}}\right)=\frac{4\rho}{(1-\rho)^{3/2}}. |
|
Dividing both sides by (1−ρ)(1-\rho) completes the proof.
∎
A.14 Proof of Lemma A.13
Lemma A.13 (Upper bound of ∑t=1T𝔼⁡[‖𝐮t‖22]\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\right]).
Under the condition that β1​(1+qM)<β2​(1−qV)\beta_{1}(1+q_{M})<\beta_{2}(1-q_{V}), the expected sum of squared updates over TT iterations is bounded by:
|
∑t=1T𝔼⁡[‖𝐮t‖22]\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\right] |
≤d(1−β1​(1+qM))​(1−β1​(1+qM)β2​(1−qV))\displaystyle\leq\frac{d}{(1-\beta_{1}(1+q_{M}))(1-\frac{\beta_{1}(1+q_{M})}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})})} |
|
|
|
×(ln⁡(1+((1+qG)​R)2ϵ⁡(1−β2​(1−qV)))−T​ln⁡(β2​(1−qV))).\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\ln\left(1+\frac{((1+q_{G})R)^{2}}{\epsilon(1-\beta_{2}(1-q_{V}))}\right)-T\ln(\beta_{2}(1-q_{V}))\right). |
|
(A.112) |
Proof.
The proof proceeds by first expanding the term of interest, applying bounds on the moment estimates derived from their recurrence relations, and then leveraging Lemma A.10 to bound the resulting sum.
We begin by expanding the definition of ‖𝐮t‖22\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}, separating the sum over the dimension dd, and taking the expectation:
|
∑t=1T𝔼⁡[‖𝐮t‖22]=∑t=1T𝔼⁡[∑i∈[d]mt,i2ϵ+vt,i]=∑i∈[d]∑t=1T𝔼⁡[mt,i2ϵ+vt,i].\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\right]=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i\in[d]}\frac{m^{2}_{t,i}}{\epsilon+v_{t,i}}\right]=\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{m^{2}_{t,i}}{\epsilon+v_{t,i}}\right]. |
|
(A.113) |
For each coordinate ii, we bound the numerator mt,i2m_{t,i}^{2} from above and the denominator ϵ+vt,i\epsilon+v_{t,i} from below. By unrolling the recurrence for mt,im_{t,i} and applying the triangle inequality along with the relative error model, we get an upper bound on its magnitude:
|
|mt,i|≤∑k=1tβ1t−k​(1+qM)t−k​|∇ifk​(𝐰k−1)+δk,i|.\displaystyle|m_{t,i}|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta_{1}^{t-k}(1+q_{M})^{t-k}|\nabla_{i}f_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k-1})+\delta_{k,i}|. |
|
(A.114) |
For the denominator, Lemma A.2 provides a lower bound for vt,iv_{t,i}:
|
vt,i≥∑k=1tβ2t−k​(1−qV)t−k​(∇ifk​(𝐰k−1)+δk,i)2.\displaystyle v_{t,i}\geq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta_{2}^{t-k}(1-q_{V})^{t-k}(\nabla_{i}f_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k-1})+\delta_{k,i})^{2}. |
|
(A.115) |
Substituting these into the sum gives the inequality:
|
∑t=1T𝔼⁡[‖𝐮t‖22]≤∑i∈[d]∑t=1T𝔼⁡[(∑k=1tβ1t−k​(1+qM)t−k​|g^k,i|)2ϵ+∑k=1tβ2t−k​(1−qV)t−k​g^k,i2],\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\right]\leq\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta_{1}^{t-k}(1+q_{M})^{t-k}|\hat{g}_{k,i}|\right)^{2}}{\epsilon+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta_{2}^{t-k}(1-q_{V})^{t-k}\hat{g}_{k,i}^{2}}\right], |
|
(A.116) |
where for brevity we denote g^k,i=∇ifk​(𝐰k−1)+δk,i\hat{g}_{k,i}=\nabla_{i}f_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k-1})+\delta_{k,i}.
The inner sum over tt for each coordinate ii in (A.116) perfectly matches the form required by Lemma A.10. To apply it, we make the following substitutions into the lemma’s notation:
•
Let the sequence (aj)j∈ℕ∗(a_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}} be ak=|g^k,i|a_{k}=|\hat{g}_{k,i}|.
•
Let the effective decay factors be β1′=β1​(1+qM)\beta^{\prime}_{1}=\beta_{1}(1+q_{M}) and β2′=β2​(1−qV)\beta^{\prime}_{2}=\beta_{2}(1-q_{V}). The lemma’s condition β1′<β2′\beta^{\prime}_{1}<\beta^{\prime}_{2} is satisfied by our assumption.
With these substitutions, the numerator term becomes (∑k=1t(β1′)t−k​ak)2=ct2\left(\sum_{k=1}^{t}(\beta^{\prime}_{1})^{t-k}a_{k}\right)^{2}=c_{t}^{2} and the sum in the denominator becomes ∑k=1t(β2′)t−k​ak2=bt\sum_{k=1}^{t}(\beta^{\prime}_{2})^{t-k}a_{k}^{2}=b_{t}. Applying Lemma A.10 to the sum over tt for a fixed ii yields:
|
∑t=1T𝔼⁡[ct2ϵ+bt]≤1(1−β1′)​(1−β1′/β2′)​(ln⁡(1+bTϵ)−T​ln⁡(β2′)).\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{c_{t}^{2}}{\epsilon+b_{t}}\right]\leq\frac{1}{(1-\beta^{\prime}_{1})(1-\beta^{\prime}_{1}/\beta^{\prime}_{2})}\left(\ln\left(1+\frac{b_{T}}{\epsilon}\right)-T\ln(\beta^{\prime}_{2})\right). |
|
(A.117) |
The final step is to find an upper bound for bTb_{T}. By definition:
|
bT=∑k=1T(β2′)T−k​ak2=∑k=1T(β2​(1−qV))T−k​g^k,i2.\displaystyle b_{T}=\sum_{k=1}^{T}(\beta^{\prime}_{2})^{T-k}a_{k}^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{T}(\beta_{2}(1-q_{V}))^{T-k}\hat{g}_{k,i}^{2}. |
|
(A.118) |
From Lemma A.6, we have a uniform bound on the quantized gradient estimator, |g^k,i|≤(1+qG)​R|\hat{g}_{k,i}|\leq(1+q_{G})R. Therefore:
|
bT\displaystyle b_{T} |
≤∑k=1T(β2​(1−qV))T−k​((1+qG)​R)2\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{T}(\beta_{2}(1-q_{V}))^{T-k}((1+q_{G})R)^{2} |
|
|
|
=((1+qG)​R)2​∑j=0T−1(β2​(1−qV))j\displaystyle=((1+q_{G})R)^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{T-1}(\beta_{2}(1-q_{V}))^{j} |
|
|
|
≤((1+qG)​R)2​11−β2​(1−qV).\displaystyle\leq((1+q_{G})R)^{2}\frac{1}{1-\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}. |
|
(A.119) |
Substituting the bound for bTb_{T} back into (A.117), and re-inserting the definitions of β1′\beta^{\prime}_{1} and β2′\beta^{\prime}_{2}, we obtain the bound for a single coordinate ii. As this bound is identical for all dd coordinates, we multiply by dd to get the final result stated in (A.112). This completes the proof.
∎
A.15 Proof of Lemma A.14 (Bound on Term M)
Lemma A.14 (Bound on Term M).
The term M, representing the accumulated quantization bias from (A.26), is bounded by:
|
M≤ηT​d​Tϵ​(1−β1)​(qG​R2+L​qW​R​‖𝐰0‖2)+ηT2​d​L​qW​U​R​T22​ϵ​(1−β1),\displaystyle M\leq\frac{\eta_{T}dT}{\sqrt{\epsilon}(1-\beta_{1})}\left(q_{G}R^{2}+Lq_{W}R\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{2}\right)+\frac{\eta_{T}^{2}dLq_{W}URT^{2}}{2\sqrt{\epsilon}(1-\beta_{1})}, |
|
(A.120) |
where U=d1−β12​(1+qM)2β2​(1−qV)U=\sqrt{\frac{d}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}(1+q_{M})^{2}}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}}}.
Proof.
First, we establish a uniform bound on the update norm ‖𝐮t‖2\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\|_{2} using Lemma A.4:
|
‖𝐮t‖2\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\|_{2} |
=∑i∈[d]mt,i2ϵ+vt,i\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{i\in[d]}\frac{m_{t,i}^{2}}{\epsilon+v_{t,i}}} |
|
|
|
≤∑i∈[d](∑k=0tβ1t−k​(1+qM)t−k​|∇ifk​(𝐰k−1)+δk,i|)2(∑k=0tβ2t−k​(1−qV)t−k​(∇ifk​(𝐰k−1)+δk,i)2)\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i\in[d]}\frac{(\sum_{k=0}^{t}\beta_{1}^{t-k}(1+q_{M})^{t-k}|\nabla_{i}f_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k-1})+\delta_{k,i}|)^{2}}{(\sum_{k=0}^{t}\beta_{2}^{t-k}(1-q_{V})^{t-k}(\nabla_{i}f_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k-1})+\delta_{k,i})^{2})}} |
|
|
|
≤d1−β12​(1+qM)2β2​(1−qV)≜U\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{d}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}(1+q_{M})^{2}}{\beta_{2}(1-q_{V})}}}\triangleq U |
|
(A.121) |
Now, let’s recall the definition of MM:
|
M=ηT​d​∑t=1T𝔼⁡[∑k=0t−1β1k​Mt−k],whereMt−k=qG​R2+L​qW​R​‖𝐰t−k−1‖2ϵ.M=\eta_{T}d\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}M_{t-k}\right],\quad\text{where}\quad M_{t-k}=\frac{q_{G}R^{2}+Lq_{W}R\|\mathbf{w}_{t-k-1}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}. |
|
We can split MM into two components: a constant part MconstM_{\text{const}} and a weight-dependent part MweightsM_{\text{weights}}.
|
Mconst\displaystyle M_{\text{const}} |
=ηT​d​qG​R2ϵ​∑t=1T∑k=0t−1β1k\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{T}dq_{G}R^{2}}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k} |
|
|
Mweights\displaystyle M_{\text{weights}} |
=ηT​d​L​qW​Rϵ​∑t=1T∑k=0t−1β1k​𝔼​[‖𝐰t−k−1‖2]\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{T}dLq_{W}R}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{w}_{t-k-1}\|_{2}\right] |
|
Bounding MconstM_{\text{const}} is straightforward. The inner sum is a geometric series bounded by 11−β1\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}, so the double summation is bounded by T1−β1\frac{T}{1-\beta_{1}}.
|
Mconst≤ηT​d​qG​R2​Tϵ​(1−β1).M_{\text{const}}\leq\frac{\eta_{T}dq_{G}R^{2}T}{\sqrt{\epsilon}(1-\beta_{1})}. |
|
(A.122) |
The main challenge is to bound MweightsM_{\text{weights}}. To do this, we first need a bound on the expected weight norm 𝔼⁡[‖𝐰j‖2]\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{w}_{j}\|_{2}\right]. From the update rule 𝐰j=𝐰j−1−ηj​𝐮j\mathbf{w}_{j}=\mathbf{w}_{j-1}-\eta_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j}, we can unroll the recursion:
|
𝐰j=𝐰0−∑l=1jηl​𝐮l.\mathbf{w}_{j}=\mathbf{w}_{0}-\sum_{l=1}^{j}\eta_{l}\mathbf{u}_{l}. |
|
Applying the triangle inequality and taking the expectation, we get:
|
𝔼⁡[‖𝐰j‖2]≤‖𝐰0‖2+𝔼⁡[∑l=1jηl​‖𝐮l‖2].\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{w}_{j}\|_{2}\right]\leq\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{j}\eta_{l}\|\mathbf{u}_{l}\|_{2}\right]. |
|
Using the uniform bound ‖𝐮l‖2≤U\|\mathbf{u}_{l}\|_{2}\leq U, we have:
|
𝔼⁡[‖𝐰j‖2]≤‖𝐰0‖2+U​∑l=1jηl≤‖𝐰0‖2+U​j​ηT.\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{w}_{j}\|_{2}\right]\leq\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{2}+U\sum_{l=1}^{j}\eta_{l}\leq\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{2}+Uj\eta_{T}. |
|
Now we substitute this bound back into the expression for MweightsM_{\text{weights}}. We first swap the order of summation. Let j=t−k−1j=t-k-1. For a fixed j∈{0,…,T−1}j\in\{0,\dots,T-1\}, the term 𝔼⁡[‖𝐰j‖2]\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{w}_{j}\|_{2}\right] appears when k=t−j−1k=t-j-1. This is valid for tt from j+1j+1 to TT.
|
∑t=1T∑k=0t−1β1k​𝔼​[‖𝐰t−k−1‖2]\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta_{1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{w}_{t-k-1}\|_{2}\right] |
=∑j=0T−1𝔼⁡[‖𝐰j‖2]​∑t=j+1Tβ1t−j−1\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{w}_{j}\|_{2}\right]\sum_{t=j+1}^{T}\beta_{1}^{t-j-1} |
|
|
|
=∑j=0T−1𝔼⁡[‖𝐰j‖2]​∑m=0T−j−1β1m\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{w}_{j}\|_{2}\right]\sum_{m=0}^{T-j-1}\beta_{1}^{m} |
|
|
|
≤11−β1​∑j=0T−1𝔼⁡[‖𝐰j‖2].\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\sum_{j=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{w}_{j}\|_{2}\right]. |
|
Next, we substitute the linear bound:
|
11−β1​∑j=0T−1𝔼⁡[‖𝐰j‖2]\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\sum_{j=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{w}_{j}\|_{2}\right] |
≤11−β1​∑j=0T−1(‖𝐰0‖2+j⋅U⋅ηT)\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\sum_{j=0}^{T-1}\left(\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{2}+j\cdot U\cdot\eta_{T}\right) |
|
|
|
=11−β1​(T​‖𝐰0‖2+U​ηT​∑j=0T−1j).\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\left(T\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{2}+U\eta_{T}\sum_{j=0}^{T-1}j\right). |
|
The sum of the first T−1T-1 integers is (T−1)​T2<T22\frac{(T-1)T}{2}<\frac{T^{2}}{2}. This gives:
|
≤11−β1​(T​‖𝐰0‖2+T22​U​ηT).\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\left(T\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{2}+\frac{T^{2}}{2}U\eta_{T}\right). |
|
Finally, we assemble the complete bound for MweightsM_{\text{weights}}:
|
Mweights≤ηT​d​L​qW​Rϵ​(1−β1)​(T​‖𝐰0‖2+T22​U​ηT).M_{\text{weights}}\leq\frac{\eta_{T}dLq_{W}R}{\sqrt{\epsilon}(1-\beta_{1})}\left(T\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{2}+\frac{T^{2}}{2}U\eta_{T}\right). |
|
Combining MconstM_{\text{const}} with MweightsM_{\text{weights}}, we get the final bound for MM.
|
M\displaystyle M |
≤ηT​d​Tϵ​(1−β1)​(qG​R2+L​qW​R​‖𝐰0‖2)+ηT2​d​L​qW​U​R​T22​ϵ​(1−β1)\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta_{T}dT}{\sqrt{\epsilon}(1-\beta_{1})}\left(q_{G}R^{2}+Lq_{W}R\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{2}\right)+\frac{\eta_{T}^{2}dLq_{W}URT^{2}}{2\sqrt{\epsilon}(1-\beta_{1})} |
|
∎

B.2 Proof of Theorem 4.6
Proof.

≥\displaystyle\geq |
𝔼⁡[⟨∇F​(𝐖t),𝐖t−𝐖t+1⟩−L2​‖𝐖t−𝐖t+1‖F2]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t}),\mathbf{W}_{t}-\mathbf{W}_{t+1}\rangle-\frac{L}{2}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}-\mathbf{W}_{t+1}\|_{F}^{2}] |

𝔼⁡[η​‖𝐌t‖∗−η​‖∇F​(𝐖t)−𝐌t‖F⋅‖𝐔t​𝐕t⊤‖F]−L2​η2​r\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\eta\|\mathbf{M}_{t}\|_{*}-\eta\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t})-\mathbf{M}_{t}\|_{F}\cdot\|\mathbf{U}_{t}\mathbf{V}_{t}^{\top}\|_{F}]-\frac{L}{2}\eta^{2}r |

(B.7) |
The second inequality is due to ‖𝐔t​𝐕t⊤‖F2=Tr(𝐕t​𝐔t⊤​𝐔t​𝐕t⊤)≤r=min⁡{m,n}\|\mathbf{U}_{t}\mathbf{V}_{t}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathbf{V}_{t}\mathbf{U}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{t}\mathbf{V}_{t}^{\top})\leq r=\min\{m,n\}. The third inequality is due to 𝐌t=𝐔t​𝐒t​𝐕t⊤\mathbf{M}_{t}=\mathbf{U}_{t}\mathbf{S}_{t}\mathbf{V}_{t}^{\top} and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. The last inequality we used the fact that ‖𝐀‖∗≤r​‖𝐀‖F\|\mathbf{A}\|_{*}\leq\sqrt{r}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F} for any 𝐀∈ℝm×n\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}.
Summing Eq. (B.7) over t=0,1,…,T−1t=0,1,\ldots,T-1, we get

𝔼[F(𝐖0)−F(𝐖T)]T​η+2​rT​∑t=0T−1𝔼⁡[‖∇F​(𝐖t)−𝐌t‖F]+η​L​r2.\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}[F(\mathbf{W}_{0})-F^{(}\mathbf{W}_{T})]}{T\eta}+\frac{2\sqrt{r}}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t})-\mathbf{M}_{t}\|_{F}]+\frac{\eta Lr}{2}. |

(B.8) |
Next, we focus on term 𝔼⁡[‖∇F​(𝐖t)−𝐌t‖F]\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t})-\mathbf{M}_{t}\|_{F}]. With auxiliary variables defined in Eq. (B.2)-(B.5), we have

β​L​η​r1−β+βt​3​σB+1−β1+β​3​σB+3​qG​(σ+G)+3​qG​T​η​r​L+qW​(1+qG)​D​L+\displaystyle\frac{\beta L\eta\sqrt{r}}{1-\beta}+\beta^{t}\frac{3\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}\frac{3\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+3q_{G}(\sigma+G)+3q_{G}T\eta\sqrt{r}L+q_{W}(1+q_{G})DL+ |

(B.9) |
Substitute (B.9) into (B.8), with Assumption 3.1, we have
