|
|
6​qG​r​(σ+G)+6​qG​T​η​r​L+2​qW​(1+qG)​D​L​r+2​qW​(1+qG)​T​η​r​L+\displaystyle 6q_{G}\sqrt{r}(\sigma+G)+6q_{G}T\eta rL+2q_{W}(1+q_{G})DL\sqrt{r}+2q_{W}(1+q_{G})T\eta rL+ |
|
|
|
2​qM​β​r1−β⁡(1+qM)​(σB+1−β1+β⋅σB+G+qG​(σ+G)+CLOSE\displaystyle\frac{2q_{M}\beta\sqrt{r}}{1-\beta(1+q_{M})}\bigg(\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}\cdot\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+G+q_{G}(\sigma+G)+\bigg. |
|
|
|
OPENqW​(1+qG)​D​L+(1+qW)​(1+qG)​T​η​r​L)\displaystyle\bigg.q_{W}(1+q_{G})DL+(1+q_{W})(1+q_{G})T\eta\sqrt{r}L\bigg) |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
𝔼⁡[F⁡(𝐖0)−F⁡(𝐖T)]η​T+L​η​r2+2​β​L​η​r1−β+6​σ​rT⁡(1−β)​B+1−β1+β​6​σ​rB+\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}[F(\mathbf{W}_{0})-F(\mathbf{W}_{T})]}{\eta T}+\frac{L\eta r}{2}+\frac{2\beta L\eta r}{1-\beta}+\frac{6\sigma\sqrt{r}}{T(1-\beta)\sqrt{B}}+\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}\frac{6\sigma\sqrt{r}}{\sqrt{B}}+ |
|
|
|
Θ⁡(qG+qW+qG​T​η+qW​T​η+qM​β1−β⁡(1+qM)​(1+1−β+qG+qW+T​η)).\displaystyle\Theta\Bigg(q_{G}+q_{W}+q_{G}T\eta+q_{W}T\eta+\frac{q_{M}\beta}{1-\beta(1+q_{M})}\bigg(1+\sqrt{1-\beta}+q_{G}+q_{W}+T\eta\bigg)\Bigg). |
|
Let F⁡(𝐖0)−F∗≤ΔF(\mathbf{W}_{0})-F^{*}\leq\Delta, where Δ>0\Delta>0 is a constant. By setting B=1B=1, 1−β=Θ(T−1/2)1-\beta=\Theta(T^{-1/2}), η=Θ((1−β)1/2T−1/2)\eta=\Theta((1-\beta)^{1/2}T^{-1/2}), we have T​η=Θ⁡(T1/4)T\eta=\Theta(T^{1/4}). Then we have
|
𝔼⁡[F⁡(𝐖0)−F⁡(𝐖T)]η​T+L​η​r2+2​β​L​η​r1−β+6​σ​rT⁡(1−β)​B+1−β1+β​6​σ​rB=𝒪⁡(1T1/4).\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}[F(\mathbf{W}_{0})-F(\mathbf{W}_{T})]}{\eta T}+\frac{L\eta r}{2}+\frac{2\beta L\eta r}{1-\beta}+\frac{6\sigma\sqrt{r}}{T(1-\beta)\sqrt{B}}+\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}\frac{6\sigma\sqrt{r}}{\sqrt{B}}=\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{T^{1/4}}). |
|
Moreover, with condition β⁡(1+qM)<1\beta(1+q_{M})<1, suppose 1−β=CβT−1/21-\beta=C_{\beta}T^{-1/2}, Cβ>0C_{\beta}>0 is a constant. Choose qM=CMT−1/2q_{M}=C_{M}T^{-1/2}, where CM​<Cβ,CM>​0C_{M}<C_{\beta},C_{M}>0 is a constant, then we have
|
β(1+qM)=(1−CβT−1/2)(1+CMT−1/2)=1−(Cβ−CM)T−1/2−CβCMT−1<1.\displaystyle\beta(1+q_{M})=(1-C_{\beta}T^{-1/2})(1+C_{M}T^{-1/2})=1-(C_{\beta}-C_{M})T^{-1/2}-C_{\beta}C_{M}T^{-1}<1. |
|
Thus, by setting qG=𝒪(T−1/2)q_{G}=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1/2}), qW=𝒪(T−1/2)q_{W}=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1/2}), qM=𝒪(T−1/2)q_{M}=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1/2}), we have
|
|
qG+qW+qG​T​η+qW​T​η+qM​β1−β⁡(1+qM)​(1+1−β+qG+qW+T​η)\displaystyle q_{G}+q_{W}+q_{G}T\eta+q_{W}T\eta+\frac{q_{M}\beta}{1-\beta(1+q_{M})}\bigg(1+\sqrt{1-\beta}+q_{G}+q_{W}+T\eta\bigg) |
|
|
=\displaystyle= |
𝒪(T−1/2+T−1/2T1/4+T−1/2(1+T−1/4+T−1/2+T1/4))\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(T^{-1/2}+T^{-1/2}T^{1/4}+T^{-1/2}(1+T^{-1/4}+T^{-1/2}+T^{1/4})) |
|
|
=\displaystyle= |
𝒪(T−1/4),\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(T^{-1/4}), |
|
where we used the fact qM​β1−β⁡(1+qM)=𝒪(qMβ(1+β(1+qM)))=𝒪(T−1/2)\frac{q_{M}\beta}{1-\beta(1+q_{M})}=\mathcal{O}(q_{M}\beta(1+\beta(1+q_{M})))=\mathcal{O}(T^{-1/2}), and β⁡(1+qM)<1\beta(1+q_{M})<1.
Combining the above results, with the fact that ‖𝐀‖∗≥‖𝐀‖F\|\mathbf{A}\|_{*}\geq\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F} for any matrix 𝐀\mathbf{A}, we complete the proof.
∎
B.3 Proof of Lemma B.1
Lemma B.1 (Bound of ‖𝐖‖F\|\mathbf{W}\|_{F} and ‖∇F​(𝐖)‖F\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W})\|_{F} for Muon).
Suppose Assumptions 4.3 and 4.4 hold. The iterates of Muon satisfy that for any t≥0t\geq 0,
|
‖𝐖t‖F≤D+t​η​r,‖∇F​(𝐖t)‖F≤G+t​η​r​L.\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}_{t}\|_{F}\leq D+t\eta\sqrt{r},\quad\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t})\|_{F}\leq G+t\eta\sqrt{r}L. |
|
Proof of Lemma B.1.
According to the update of Muon, we have
|
|
‖𝐖t‖F\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}_{t}\|_{F} |
|
|
=\displaystyle= |
‖𝐖t−1−η​𝐔t​𝐕t⊤‖F\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}_{t-1}-\eta\mathbf{U}_{t}\mathbf{V}_{t}^{\top}\|_{F} |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
‖𝐖t−1‖F+η​‖𝐔t​𝐕t⊤‖F\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}_{t-1}\|_{F}+\eta\|\mathbf{U}_{t}\mathbf{V}_{t}^{\top}\|_{F} |
|
|
=\displaystyle= |
‖𝐖t−1‖F+η​Tr(𝐕t​𝐔t⊤​𝐔t​𝐕t⊤)\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}_{t-1}\|_{F}+\eta\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathbf{V}_{t}\mathbf{U}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{t}\mathbf{V}_{t}^{\top})} |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
‖𝐖t−1‖F+η​r\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}_{t-1}\|_{F}+\eta\sqrt{r} |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
‖𝐖0‖F+t​η​r\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\|_{F}+t\eta\sqrt{r} |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
D+t​η​r.\displaystyle D+t\eta\sqrt{r}. |
|
The third inequality is because 𝐔t\mathbf{U}_{t} and 𝐕t\mathbf{V}_{t} are orthogonal matrices, and the last inequality is due to Assumption 4.4.
|
|
‖∇F​(𝐖t)‖F\displaystyle\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t})\|_{F} |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
‖∇F​(𝐖0)‖F+∑k=0t−1‖∇F​(𝐖k+1)−∇F​(𝐖k)‖F\displaystyle\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{0})\|_{F}+\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{k+1})-\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{k})\|_{F} |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
G+∑k=0t−1L​‖𝐖k+1−𝐖k‖F\displaystyle G+\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}L\|\mathbf{W}_{k+1}-\mathbf{W}_{k}\|_{F} |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
G+∑k=0t−1L​η​r\displaystyle G+\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}L\eta\sqrt{r} |
|
|
=\displaystyle= |
G+t​η​r​L.\displaystyle G+t\eta\sqrt{r}L. |
|
The first inequality is due to the triangle inequality, the second inequality is due to Assumption 4.3, and the last inequality is due to the update of Muon.
∎
B.4 Proof of Lemma B.2
Lemma B.2.
Suppose Assumption 3.1 holds. For any matrix 𝐗∈ℝm×n\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n} and its quantized version 𝐗Q\mathbf{X}^{Q}, we have
|
‖𝐗Q−𝐗‖F≤q​‖𝐗‖F.\displaystyle\|\mathbf{X}^{Q}-\mathbf{X}\|_{F}\leq q\|\mathbf{X}\|_{F}. |
|
Proof of Lemma B.2.
According to Assumption 3.1, we have
|
‖𝐗Q−𝐗‖F2=\displaystyle\|\mathbf{X}^{Q}-\mathbf{X}\|_{F}^{2}= |
∑i=1m∑j=1n|Xi​jQ−Xi​j|2\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|X_{ij}^{Q}-X_{ij}|^{2} |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
∑i=1m∑j=1nq2​|Xi​j|2\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{n}q^{2}|X_{ij}|^{2} |
|
|
=\displaystyle= |
q2​‖𝐗‖F2.\displaystyle q^{2}\|\mathbf{X}\|_{F}^{2}. |
|
Taking the square root on both sides, we complete the proof.
∎
B.5 Proof of Lemma B.3
Lemma B.3.
Suppose Assumptions 4.3 and 4.4 hold. For any t≥0t\geq 0, we have
|
𝔼⁡[‖∇F​(𝐖t)−𝐂t‖F]≤β​L​η​r1−β.\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t})-\mathbf{C}_{t}\|_{F}]\leq\frac{\beta L\eta\sqrt{r}}{1-\beta}. |
|
Proof of Lemma B.3.
This proof is a standard technique for bounding the bias term of momentum. We have
|
|
𝔼⁡[‖∇F​(𝐖t)−𝐂t‖F]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t})-\mathbf{C}_{t}\|_{F}] |
|
|
=\displaystyle= |
𝔼[∥∇F(𝐖t)−(β𝐂t−1+(1−β)∇F(𝐖t))∥F]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t})-(\beta\mathbf{C}_{t-1}+(1-\beta)\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t}))\|_{F}] |
|
|
=\displaystyle= |
𝔼⁡[β​‖∇F​(𝐖t)−𝐂t−1‖F]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\beta\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t})-\mathbf{C}_{t-1}\|_{F}] |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
𝔼⁡[β​‖∇F​(𝐖t−1)−𝐂t−1‖F+β​‖∇F​(𝐖t−1)−∇F​(𝐖t)‖F]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\beta\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t-1})-\mathbf{C}_{t-1}\|_{F}+\beta\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t-1})-\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t})\|_{F}] |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
𝔼⁡[β​‖∇F​(𝐖t−1)−𝐂t−1‖F+β​L​‖𝐖t−1−𝐖t‖F]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\beta\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t-1})-\mathbf{C}_{t-1}\|_{F}+\beta L\|\mathbf{W}_{t-1}-\mathbf{W}_{t}\|_{F}] |
|
|
=\displaystyle= |
𝔼⁡[β​‖∇F​(𝐖t−1)−𝐂t−1‖F+β​L​η​‖𝐔t−1​𝐕t−1⊤‖F]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\beta\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t-1})-\mathbf{C}_{t-1}\|_{F}+\beta L\eta\|\mathbf{U}_{t-1}\mathbf{V}_{t-1}^{\top}\|_{F}] |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
𝔼⁡[β​‖∇F​(𝐖t−1)−𝐂t−1‖F+β​L​η​r]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\beta\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t-1})-\mathbf{C}_{t-1}\|_{F}+\beta L\eta\sqrt{r}] |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
βt​‖∇F​(𝐖0)−𝐂0‖F+∑i=1tβi​L​η​r\displaystyle\beta^{t}\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{0})-\mathbf{C}_{0}\|_{F}+\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta^{i}L\eta\sqrt{r} |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
β​L​η​r1−β.\displaystyle\frac{\beta L\eta\sqrt{r}}{1-\beta}. |
|
∎
B.6 Proof of Lemma B.4
Lemma B.4.
Suppose Assumptions 4.1 and 4.2 hold. For any t≥0t\geq 0, we have
|
𝔼⁡[‖𝐂t−𝐗t‖F]≤βt​σB+1−β1+β​σB.\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{C}_{t}-\mathbf{X}_{t}\|_{F}]\leq\beta^{t}\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}. |
|
Proof of Lemma B.4.
Expanding 𝐂t\mathbf{C}_{t} and 𝐗t\mathbf{X}_{t} by their definitions in (B.2) and (B.3), we have
|
𝐂t=\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{t}= |
βt𝐂0+(1−β)∑k=1tβt−k∇F(𝐖k),\displaystyle\beta^{t}\mathbf{C}_{0}+(1-\beta)\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta^{t-k}\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{k}), |
|
|
𝐗t=\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{t}= |
βt​𝐗0+(1−β)​∑k=1tβt−k​1B​∑i=1B∇f​(𝐖k,𝝃k,i).\displaystyle\beta^{t}\mathbf{X}_{0}+(1-\beta)\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta^{t-k}\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^{B}\nabla f(\mathbf{W}_{k};\bm{\xi}_{k,i}). |
|
Thus, we have
|
|
𝔼⁡[‖𝐂t−𝐗t‖F]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{C}_{t}-\mathbf{X}_{t}\|_{F}] |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
𝔼⁡[‖βt​(𝐂0−𝐗0)‖F]+𝔼⁡[(1−β)​‖∑k=1tβt−k​(∇F​(𝐖k)−1B​∑i=1B∇f​(𝐖k,𝝃k,i))‖F]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\beta^{t}(\mathbf{C}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0})\|_{F}]+\mathbb{E}[(1-\beta)\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta^{t-k}(\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{k})-\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^{B}\nabla f(\mathbf{W}_{k};\bm{\xi}_{k,i}))\|_{F}] |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
βt​𝔼​[‖𝐂0−𝐗0‖F]+𝔼⁡[(1−β)2​‖∑k=1tβt−k​(∇F​(𝐖k)−1B​∑i=1B∇f​(𝐖k,𝝃k,i))‖F2]\displaystyle\beta^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{C}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\|_{F}]+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(1-\beta)^{2}\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta^{t-k}(\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{k})-\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^{B}\nabla f(\mathbf{W}_{k};\bm{\xi}_{k,i}))\|_{F}^{2}]} |
|
|
=\displaystyle= |
βt​𝔼​[‖𝐂0−𝐗0‖F]+𝔼⁡[(1−β)2​∑k=1tβ2​(t−k)​1B2​∑i=1B‖∇F​(𝐖k,𝝃k,i)−∇F​(𝐖k)‖F2]\displaystyle\beta^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{C}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\|_{F}]+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(1-\beta)^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta^{2(t-k)}\frac{1}{B^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{B}\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{k};\bm{\xi}_{k,i})-\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{k})\|_{F}^{2}]} |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
βt​𝔼​[‖𝐂0−𝐗0‖F]+(1−β)2​∑k=1tβ2​(t−k)​σ2B\displaystyle\beta^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{C}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\|_{F}]+\sqrt{(1-\beta)^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta^{2(t-k)}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{B}} |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
βt​σB+1−β1+β​σB.\displaystyle\beta^{t}\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}. |
|
The second inequality is due to Jensen’s inequality, the first equality is due to the independence of 𝝃k,i\bm{\xi}_{k,i} for different kk or ii, and the third inequality is due to Assumptions 4.1 and 4.2.
∎
B.7 Proof of Lemma B.5
Lemma B.5.
Suppose Assumptions 4.1, 4.2 and 3.1 hold. For any t≥0t\geq 0, we have
|
𝔼⁡[‖𝐗t−𝐘t‖F]≤qG​(σ+G+t​η​r​L).\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}_{t}-\mathbf{Y}_{t}\|_{F}]\leq q_{G}(\sigma+G+t\eta\sqrt{r}L). |
|
Proof of Lemma B.5.
By the definition of 𝐗t\mathbf{X}_{t} and 𝐘t\mathbf{Y}_{t} in (B.3) and (B.4), we have
|
|
𝔼⁡[‖𝐗t−𝐘t‖F]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}_{t}-\mathbf{Y}_{t}\|_{F}] |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
𝔼⁡[β​‖𝐗t−1−𝐘t−1‖F]+(1−β)​1B​∑i=1B𝔼⁡[‖∇f​(𝐖t,𝝃t,i)−∇Qf​(𝐖t,𝝃t,i)‖F]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\beta\|\mathbf{X}_{t-1}-\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}\|_{F}]+(1-\beta)\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^{B}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f(\mathbf{W}_{t};\bm{\xi}_{t,i})-\nabla^{Q}f(\mathbf{W}_{t};\bm{\xi}_{t,i})\|_{F}] |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
𝔼⁡[β​‖𝐗t−1−𝐘t−1‖F]+(1−β)​1B​∑i=1B𝔼⁡[qG​‖∇f​(𝐖t,𝝃t,i)‖F]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\beta\|\mathbf{X}_{t-1}-\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}\|_{F}]+(1-\beta)\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^{B}\mathbb{E}[q_{G}\|\nabla f(\mathbf{W}_{t};\bm{\xi}_{t,i})\|_{F}] |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
𝔼⁡[β​‖𝐗t−1−𝐘t−1‖F]+(1−β)​𝔼​[qG​(σ+‖∇F​(𝐖t)‖F)]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\beta\|\mathbf{X}_{t-1}-\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}\|_{F}]+(1-\beta)\mathbb{E}[q_{G}(\sigma+\|\nabla F(\mathbf{W}_{t})\|_{F})] |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
𝔼⁡[β​‖𝐗t−1−𝐘t−1‖F]+(1−β)​qG​(σ+G+t​η​r​L)\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\beta\|\mathbf{X}_{t-1}-\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}\|_{F}]+(1-\beta)q_{G}(\sigma+G+t\eta\sqrt{r}L) |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
βt​‖𝐗0−𝐘0‖F+(1−β)​qG​(σ+G+t​η​r​L)​∑k=0t−1βk\displaystyle\beta^{t}\|\mathbf{X}_{0}-\mathbf{Y}_{0}\|_{F}+(1-\beta)q_{G}(\sigma+G+t\eta\sqrt{r}L)\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\beta^{k} |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
βt​qG​(σ+G)+(1−βt)​qG​(σ+G+t​η​r​L)\displaystyle\beta^{t}q_{G}(\sigma+G)+(1-\beta^{t})q_{G}(\sigma+G+t\eta\sqrt{r}L) |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
qG​(σ+G)+(1−βt)​qG​t​η​r​L\displaystyle q_{G}(\sigma+G)+(1-\beta^{t})q_{G}t\eta\sqrt{r}L |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
qG​(σ+G+t​η​r​L).\displaystyle q_{G}(\sigma+G+t\eta\sqrt{r}L). |
|
The second inequality is due to Assumption 3.1, Lemma B.2 and Definition B.6. The third inequality is due to Assumption 4.2. The fourth inequality is due to Lemma B.1.
∎
B.8 Proof of Lemma B.6
Lemma B.6.
Suppose Assumptions 4.1, 4.2, 4.3 and 3.1 hold. For any t≥0t\geq 0, we have
|
𝔼⁡[‖𝐘t−𝐙t‖F]≤\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{Y}_{t}-\mathbf{Z}_{t}\|_{F}]\leq |
βt⋅2​σB+1−β1+β⋅2​σB+2​qG​(σ+G)+2​qG​t​η​r​L+\displaystyle\beta^{t}\cdot\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}\cdot\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+2q_{G}(\sigma+G)+2q_{G}t\eta\sqrt{r}L+ |
|
|
|
qW​(1+qG)​D​L+qW​(1+qG)​t​η​r​L,\displaystyle q_{W}(1+q_{G})DL+q_{W}(1+q_{G})t\eta\sqrt{r}L, |
|
|
𝔼⁡[‖𝐙t‖F]≤\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{Z}_{t}\|_{F}]\leq |
σB+1−β1+β⋅σB+G+qG​(σ+G)+qW​(1+qG)​D​L+\displaystyle\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}\cdot\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+G+q_{G}(\sigma+G)+q_{W}(1+q_{G})DL+ |
|
|
|
(1+qW)​(1+qG)​t​η​r​L.\displaystyle(1+q_{W})(1+q_{G})t\eta\sqrt{r}L. |
|
Proof of Lemma B.6.
By the definition of 𝐘t\mathbf{Y}_{t} and 𝐙t\mathbf{Z}_{t} in (B.4) and (B.5), we have
|
𝐘t=\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}_{t}= |
βt​𝐘0+(1−β)​∑k=1tβt−k​1B​∑i=1B∇Qf​(𝐖k,𝝃k,i),\displaystyle\beta^{t}\mathbf{Y}_{0}+(1-\beta)\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta^{t-k}\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^{B}\nabla^{Q}f(\mathbf{W}_{k};\bm{\xi}_{k,i}), |
|
|
𝐙t=\displaystyle\mathbf{Z}_{t}= |
βt​𝐙0+(1−β)​∑k=1tβt−k​1B​∑i=1B∇Qf​(𝐖kQ,𝝃k,i).\displaystyle\beta^{t}\mathbf{Z}_{0}+(1-\beta)\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta^{t-k}\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^{B}\nabla^{Q}f(\mathbf{W}_{k}^{Q};\bm{\xi}_{k,i}). |
|
Thus, by the triangle inequality, we have
|
|
𝔼⁡[‖𝐘t−𝐙t‖F]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{Y}_{t}-\mathbf{Z}_{t}\|_{F}] |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |
𝔼⁡[βt​‖𝐘0−𝐙0‖F]+(1−β)​𝔼​[‖∑k=1tβt−k⋅(1B​∑i=1B∇Qf​(𝐖k,𝝃k,i)−∇Qf​(𝐖kQ,𝝃k,i))‖F]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\beta^{t}\|\mathbf{Y}_{0}-\mathbf{Z}_{0}\|_{F}]+(1-\beta)\mathbb{E}[\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta^{t-k}\cdot(\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^{B}\nabla^{Q}f(\mathbf{W}_{k};\bm{\xi}_{k,i})-\nabla^{Q}f(\mathbf{W}_{k}^{Q};\bm{\xi}_{k,i}))\|_{F}] |

≤\displaystyle\leq |

(B.10) |
Next, we bound each term in (B.10) one by one.
Bound on βt​𝔼​[‖𝐘0−𝐙0‖F]\beta^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{Y}_{0}-\mathbf{Z}_{0}\|_{F}].
By the definitions of 𝐘0\mathbf{Y}_{0} and 𝐙0\mathbf{Z}_{0} in (B.4) and (B.5), we have
|
𝐘0=\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}_{0}= |
1B​∑i=1B∇Qf​(𝐖0,𝝃0,i),\displaystyle\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^{B}\nabla^{Q}f(\mathbf{W}_{0};\bm{\xi}_{0,i}), |
|
|
𝐙0=\displaystyle\mathbf{Z}_{0}= |

Thus, we have

|
βt​𝔼​[‖𝐘0−𝐙0‖F]\displaystyle\beta^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{Y}_{0}-\mathbf{Z}_{0}\|_{F}] |
|
|
=\displaystyle= |
βt​𝔼​[‖1B​∑i=1B∇Qf​(𝐖0,𝝃0,i)−1B​∑i=1B∇Qf​(𝐖0Q,𝝃0,i)‖F]\displaystyle\beta^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^{B}\nabla^{Q}f(\mathbf{W}_{0};\bm{\xi}_{0,i})-\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^{B}\nabla^{Q}f(\mathbf{W}_{0}^{Q};\bm{\xi}_{0,i})\|_{F}] |
|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |

|

|

≤\displaystyle\leq |
βt​1B​∑i=1BqG​𝔼​[‖∇f​(𝐖0,𝝃0,i)‖F]+βt​σB+βt​L​qW​𝔼​[‖𝐖0‖F]+βt​σB+\displaystyle\beta^{t}\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^{B}q_{G}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f(\mathbf{W}_{0};\bm{\xi}_{0,i})\|_{F}]+\beta^{t}\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+\beta^{t}Lq_{W}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\|_{F}]+\beta^{t}\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+ |

(B.11) |
The first inequality is due to the triangle inequality. The second inequality we used Definition B.6 for the first and last terms, Assumption 4.1, 4.2 and Jensen’s inequality for the second and fourth terms, and Assumption 4.3 and Definition B.6 for the third term. The third inequality is due to Assumption 4.2, 4.4 and Definition B.6.

The first inequality is due to Definition B.6 and the triangle inequality. The second inequality is due to Assumption 4.2. The third inequality is due to Assumption 4.3 and the triangle inequality. The fourth inequality is due to Definition B.6. The fifth inequality is due to Lemma B.1.
Bound on 𝔼⁡[‖𝐘t−𝐙t‖F]\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{Y}_{t}-\mathbf{Z}_{t}\|_{F}].

βt⋅2​σB+1−β1+β⋅2​σB+2​qG​(σ+G)+(1−βt)​2​qG​t​η​r​L+\displaystyle\beta^{t}\cdot\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}\cdot\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{B}}+2q_{G}(\sigma+G)+(1-\beta^{t})2q_{G}t\eta\sqrt{r}L+ |

Bound on 𝔼⁡[‖𝐙t‖F]\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{Z}_{t}\|_{F}].

|
|
≤\displaystyle\leq |

Appendix C Additional Experiments and Details
C.1 Imitating Quantization and Dequantization
We emulate floating-point quantization and dequantization by reducing the mantissa length from its original precision (52 bits for float64 and 23 bits for float32) to MM bits, while keeping the exponent and sign bits unchanged.
This design choice is motivated by the fact that practical scaling techniques can effectively prevent overflow and underflow [Peng et al., 2023].
After truncating the mantissa, we apply stochastic rounding to the nearest two representable values, and then dequantize the result back to standard float32 or float64.
C.2 Synthetic Experiments
We conduct synthetic experiments on the Rosenbrock function, defined as
|
F⁡(𝐖)=∑j=1n−1(100​‖𝐖j+1−𝐖j2‖F2+‖𝟏m−𝐖j‖F2),\displaystyle F(\mathbf{W})=\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\Big(100\|\mathbf{W}_{j+1}-\mathbf{W}_{j}^{2}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\mathbf{1}_{m}-\mathbf{W}_{j}\|_{F}^{2}\Big), |
|
where 𝐖=[𝐖1,𝐖2,…,𝐖d]∈ℝm×n\mathbf{W}=[\mathbf{W}_{1},\mathbf{W}_{2},\dots,\mathbf{W}_{d}]\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n} is the weight matrix.
The global minimum is at 𝐖∗=[𝟏m,𝟏m,…,𝟏m]\mathbf{W}^{*}=[\mathbf{1}_{m},\mathbf{1}_{m},\dots,\mathbf{1}_{m}] with F⁡(𝐖∗)=0F(\mathbf{W}^{*})=0.
We set m=50m=50, d=100d=100, and initialize 𝐖0∼𝒩⁡(𝟏m×n,0.12​𝐈)\mathbf{W}_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{1}_{m\times n},0.1^{2}\mathbf{I}).
For Muon, we apply the default hyperparameters in the Newton-Schulz iteration to compute the zeroth power / orthogonalization of GG [Jordan et al., 2024], using double precision.
Figure 3 shows the gradient norms of Adam with different quantization errors on the Rosenbrock function. Figure 4 shows the gradient norms of Muon with different quantization errors on the Rosenbrock function.
Figure 5 shows the function values of Adam with different quantization errors on the Rosenbrock function. Figure 6 shows the function values of Muon with different quantization errors on the Rosenbrock function. The relative quantization error is defined as ‖𝐗−Q⁡(𝐗)‖F‖𝐗‖F\frac{\|\mathbf{X}-Q(\mathbf{X})\|_{F}}{\|\mathbf{X}\|_{F}}, measuring the average quantization error of 𝐗\mathbf{X}, where Q⁡(⋅)Q(\cdot) is the quantization operator.
Figure 5: Rosenbrock: Adam relative quantization error of different mantissa bits (MM). Weights error (top left), Gradient error (top right), First moment error (bottom left), Second moment error (bottom right). These results show that the more mantissa bits, the smaller the relative quantization error. Combining with Figure 3, we can see that the more mantissa bits, the smaller quantization error, the better convergence performance (Theorem 4.5).
Figure 6: Rosenbrock: Muon relative quantization error of different mantissa bits (MM). Weights error (left), Gradient error (middle), Momentum error (right). These results show that the more mantissa bits, the smaller the relative quantization error. Combining with Figure 4, we can see that the more mantissa bits, the smaller quantization error, the better convergence performance (Theorem 4.6).
C.3 Real-data Experiments
We conduct real-data experiments on the CIFAR-10 dataset [Krizhevsky et al., 2009] using a 4-layer fully connected network (FCN).
The architecture is as follows: an input layer with 3072 neurons (corresponding to 32×32×332\times 32\times 3 images), followed by three hidden layers with 512, 256, and 64 neurons, respectively, and an output layer with 10 neurons for classification.
ReLU activations are used for all hidden layers, and the network is trained with the cross-entropy loss for 100 epochs.
We evaluate both Adam and Muon under varying quantization precisions.
For Adam, we use mantissa bit-lengths M∈{1,2,3,7,10,23}M\in\{1,2,3,7,10,23\}, batch size B=256B=256, learning rate η=1.5×10−4\eta=1.5\times 10^{-4}, β1=0.95\beta_{1}=0.95, β2=0.999\beta_{2}=0.999, ϵ=10−8\epsilon=10^{-8}, and weight decay 0.10.1.
For Muon, vector parameters are updated using Adam, while matrix parameters are updated with Muon’s orthogonalization step.
We choose mantissa bit-lengths M∈{2,3,7,10,23}M\in\{2,3,7,10,23\}, batch size B=512B=512, learning rate η=0.001\eta=0.001, β=0.99\beta=0.99, weight decay 0.10.1, and 5 Newton–Schulz iterations, following the iteration hyperparameters in Jordan et al. [2024]. The auxiliary Adam optimizer in Muon uses learning rate η=2×10−4\eta=2\times 10^{-4}, β1=0.9\beta_{1}=0.9, β2=0.999\beta_{2}=0.999, ϵ=10−8\epsilon=10^{-8}, and weight decay 0.050.05.
Figure 7 shows the gradient norms of Adam with different quantization errors on CIFAR-10. Figure 8 shows the gradient norms of Muon with different quantization errors on CIFAR-10.
Figure 9 shows the quantization errors of Adam with different precision on CIFAR-10. Figure 10 shows the quantization errors of Muon with different precision on CIFAR-10. The relative quantization error is defined as ‖𝐗−Q⁡(𝐗)‖F‖𝐗‖F\frac{\|\mathbf{X}-Q(\mathbf{X})\|_{F}}{\|\mathbf{X}\|_{F}}, measuring the average quantization error of 𝐗\mathbf{X}, where Q⁡(⋅)Q(\cdot) is the quantization operator.
Figure 7: CIFAR-10: Adam gradient norms under different mantissa precisions MM. Larger mantissa bit-lengths lead to smaller converged gradient norms. Together with Figure 9, this demonstrates that higher precision reduces quantization error and improves convergence, consistent with Theorem 4.5.
Figure 8: CIFAR-10: Muon gradient norms under different mantissa precisions MM. Larger mantissa bit-lengths lead to smaller converged gradient norms. Together with Figure 10, this demonstrates that higher precision reduces quantization error and improves convergence, consistent with Theorem 4.6.
Figure 9: CIFAR-10: Adam relative quantization error of different mantissa bits (MM). Weights error (top left), Gradient error (top right), First moment error (bottom left), Second moment error (bottom right). These results show that the more mantissa bits, the smaller the relative quantization error. Combining with Figure 7, we can see that the more mantissa bits, the smaller quantization error, the better convergence performance (Theorem 4.5).
Figure 10: CIFAR-10: Muon with auxiliary Adam relative quantization error of different mantissa bits (MM). Weights error (top left), Gradient error (top middle), Momentum error (top right), Auxiliary Adam first moment error (bottom left), Auxiliary Adam second moment error (bottom middle). These results show that the more mantissa bits, the smaller the relative quantization error. Combining with Figure 8, we can see that the more mantissa bits, the smaller quantization error, the better convergence performance (Theorem 4.6).
